                                         FOREWORD

Mark 16:15 “Go into all the world ….”

I’ve just had the privilege of meeting a dynamic young man of
God whom I believe the Lord is going to use very powerfully on
the continent of Africa, starting in South Africa, and then going
right up into Central and North Africa. Grant visited me and
shared his vision, which is huge. At Shalom we say if your vision
doesn’t scare you, it’s not big enough. Well, I think it’s with much
fear and trepidation that Grant is looking forward to this vision
that God has given him to be fulfilled.

Being a farmer myself, I can really relate to the heart that God has given to Grant, whom I
had the privilege of meeting at Shalom some years back. It’s wonderful to see how God is
raising up men all over our continent who are reaching out to the poor and the needy.
Instead of giving them a fish, they are teaching them how to fish for themselves. Self-
respect is a very important part of a man’s life. If a man can put bread on the table for his
family, it is an honourable thing and something which God is pleased with. This is exactly
what these brave young men and women are doing, teaching people not only how to
sustain themselves, but also to make a living out of farming the land God’s way.

I really pray God’s richest blessing on Grant, his ministry, his wife, Nikki, and their children,
Benjamin and Caleb, that God would protect them as ‘they attempt great things for God
and expect great things from God’ (William Carey, founder of the Baptist Union).

It gives me great pleasure to endorse this work and to give it my fullest support. Our prayer
is that we don’t only minister to the spiritual part of man, but the mental and physical part
as well. To bring dignity to a man is a wonderful thing, especially to do it in the name of
Jesus Christ, the greatest farmer who has ever lived. I pray that this work would continue
to grow and be supported financially and mostly in prayer by God’s people as these brave
soldiers go into the most needy areas of our continent and preach God’s Gospel in a
practical way. Francis of Assisi said, “Preach the Gospel at all costs and, if you really have
to, use words.” This is what Grant is doing. He’s preaching God’s Word in a practical way.
May God bless them richly as they continue to be obedient to the vision and the call that
He has put on their lives.


Angus Buchan
Shalom Ministries
October 2022
1. Farming God's Way Values

  1. God and the Bible are central to all we teach and do. Our hope is to see lives transformed
     through the promised abundant life of Jesus Christ.

  2. Farming God's Way is rooted in a deep, heartfelt consideration for the poor, based on our
     founding scripture Isaiah 58.

  3. Farming God's Way is not just a technology. Instead it is a tool for equipping into the
     "agricultural domain" with three integrated spheres of influence: Biblical, Management and
     Technology.

  4. Farming God's Way is an expression of the good news of the Kingdom of God which
     transcends and supercedes all cultures. It is non-organisational, non-denominational and
     apolitical and is overseen by the Stewardship team.

  5. Farming God's Way is made up of a relational network of people; who voluntarily work
     under their own coverings, liberally extending this free resource into the communities they
     serve. The network is encouraged to provide regular feedback to regional leaders for co-
     ordination.

  6. Farming God's Way has an intensive equipping track to develop highly skilled accredited
     trainers, to represent Farming God's Way at the highest level and to assist in the mentoring
     of future trainers.

  7. Farming God’s Way discourages the perpetuation of the dependency cycle through
     handouts, handups or cash loans, except in the case of urgent short term disaster relief.
     Instead, we encourage the empowerment of the poor with the knowledge to be able to
     come out of poverty with what God has put in their hands.




                                            1
2. Introduction

The Lord Jesus prayed “Let Your Kingdom come on earth as it is in heaven”. His heartfelt desire is
that we, God’s people, who are called by His name, will walk in the fullness of His promised
abundant life (John 10:10b). The question is, if so much of Africa call themselves Christians, why
do we still see so many of the poor living under the curse, rather than the promise of His blessing?

   2.1. The Poverty Crisis
Worldwide, there is a desperate poverty and food insecurity problem, due to poor agricultural
practises, rising input costs, climate change and variability, population growth, government
negligence, conflict, a growing biofuel industry at the expense of food supply, and food wastage
amongst other reasons.
There are more than 800 million people worldwide who are undernourished, which is a staggering
1 in every 10 people!!! The African statistics are significantly worse, with 1 in every 5 Africans not
able to feed themselves. (1 - 2021 United Nations Statistics)




To put this map in perspective, the “Hunger Index” rated “alarming”, would equate to
undernourishment levels of 25- 35%; and the “extremely alarming” index would be
undernourishment levels of over 35%. In some of the poorer countries the reality is that 1 in every
3 people, and as severe as 1 in every 2 people, do not have enough food to sustain themselves.
This is a seriously desperate crisis and we as God’s people need to catch the heart of God for the
poor.
The worldwide food shortages are mostly met by the supply of relief grains, amounting to millions
of tons annually, and distributed through aid organizations, such as the WFP, USAID, CFGB and
many other non-governmental organizations. The USA is the world’s largest relief donor, giving
3,65 billion dollars to relief in 2020, with Germany coming in second with 1,18 billion dollars.
(3 - https://www.wfp.org/funding/2020)


                                               2
This worldwide hunger index map also shows that the African continent has by far the most
countries with severe food insecurity levels; however, considering Africa’s size and her vast natural
resources, it remains a mystery for most people as to why the continent is stuck in poverty.




                                   African Size in perspective     (4 – The Times Atlas)

Africa has an area of 30,3 million km² and is larger than the combination of China (9,6 million km²),
the USA (9,4 million km²), Western Europe (4,9 million km²), India (3,2 million km²) and Argentina
(2,8 million km²), three Scandinavian countries and the British Isles put together.




                 UN Graph of World Regional GDP as Percentage of the World’s GDP (5)

In 2020, the gross domestic product (GDP normalised estimate) of Africa was US$ 2.6 trillion
compared to the world GDP of US$ 91.98 trillion. Africa produced a mere 2.8% of the world’s GDP,
which simply highlights the continent’s vast untapped potential. The African continent has the most
natural resources of all the world’s continents. It has vast mineral, precious metal and oil reserves,
excellent agricultural potential, wonderful people, plenty of water and riverine systems and a
wildlife biodiversity, which has the potential to become a booming tourist industry.


                                               3
However, in contrast to her potential, Africa is also the most poverty stricken, with extremely low
standards of living, famines, high levels of undernourishment, shocking infant mortality rates,
disease, wars, dependency, poor education, erosion, deforestation, corruption and high inflation as
hallmarks of what the continent represents. It is no surprise that the world looks at Africa
disdainfully and the continent is cursed frequently as “the begging bowl” and “the dark continent”.
Africa’s beauty is as glorious as her poverty is ominous.

   2.2. The Failing Agricultural Sector
One of the biggest sectors of untapped potential in developing countries worldwide, is agriculture.
Agriculture should provide national food security whilst employing the highest percentages of the
population. In Africa, more than 45% of the population is involved in agriculture. However, because
of the many failings of conventional farming practices and the negative societal stigma of farming,
the percentage of people working in the agricultural sector is declining annually. Year after year the
rural poor stream to urban centres in the hope of finding employment. However, urbanization itself
is fraught with its own problems, including social vices such as crime, prostitution, substance
abuse, health hazards, HIV/Aids, poor standards of living and broken families. There is insufficient
development in the industrial, mining and commercial sectors to absorb this urbanisation trend,
which is causing the national unemployment levels to climb steadily and putting the already weak
economies under further pressure. South Africa for example has a staggering >35%
unemployment rate, while vast agronomically suitable land is lying unutilised. Urbanization is not
the solution and, on the contrary, has proven to be even more problematic than the rural dilemma.
We need to understand the root cause of the problem to be able to deal with some of these macro-
economic issues. In America, national maize yields consistently average around 11.5 tons/ha; in
South Africa, the 10 year commercial rainfed average maize yields are around 4.6t/ha, with
irrigated averages over 11t/ha; however, the African average maize yields are estimated at under 2
t/ha. That said, even this 2t/ha figure is really just an estimate of commercial agricultural yields and
does not take into account the fact that small scale subsistence farmers who make up >45% of the
African population, achieve nowhere near that yield. (6)
Over the past 20 years the author has collected yield data from small scale farmers practising
conventional farming, and found their maize yields ranging from under 0.35 - 0.8 t/ha depending on
regions. An average family of six needs approximately 1,250kg of maize to survive; however, the
annual production levels are as low as 350kg per hectare in places. This annual family shortfall
creates a crisis of food insecurity, which has to be supplemented by hunter-gathering practices,
food aid or other forms of income.
In many cases, the poor are frequently found to be eating a meal every 2nd or even 3rd day, whilst
trying to supplement their diets with ground tree leaves from indigenous trees. Some families in
Malawi and Mozambique survive on wild bulbs during difficult years, which are not suitable for
human consumption and which have no nutritional value whatsoever except to fill a stomach with
fibre. They collect this free food called “Nyika” from the submerged reeds and mud; however,
because of the many crocodiles and hippopotamuses in the floodplains, there is considerable risk
to human life and limb. Many people are maimed in the process - losing limbs, muscles and
breasts in the struggle to escape the crocodiles. Some African people groups eat rats and mice as
their sole protein supplement, burning down hundreds of thousands of hectares of natural
indigenous vegetation each year, to extract these rodents.
Children across the world are the hardest hit by hunger and malnutrition, with over 3 million
children dying of hunger every year. Malnutrition in children can lead to physical deformities with
more than 151 million children under 5 years old worldwide having stunted growth. Malnutrition
during a child’s essential formative years can also result in reduced cognitive performance, and
even retarded brain and neurological development. Not only is it essential to have enough food for
a child’s development, a well-balanced diet is just as important.




                                               4
The environment is also suffering under huge pressure from mankind, with soil erosion, siltation,
eutrophication and deforestation as some of the devastating results of the failing agricultural
sector.
         Romans 8:22 “For we know that the whole of creation groans and suffers the pains of
         childbirth together until now.”
In Africa, soil erosion predominantly due to poor agricultural land use practises, accounts for an
estimated annual average soil loss of 55 to 250 tons per hectare [7,8,9,10,11]. These enormous
topsoil losses through water and wind erosion leave behind only subsoil, which is even more
vulnerable to erosional scarring and gully formation.
Prof Heinz Beckedahl from the University of KZN estimates that the annual soil erosion loss in
South Africa amount to 500 million tons/year. That soil loss would fill a goods train 120,000km long
and be able to go around the circumference of the earth 3 times!
The knock on effect of this erosion results in the siltation of water courses, rivers, dams and
estuaries. The soil’s poor condition leads to farmers needing to over fertilise in order to
compensate for yield decline. The extra fertiliser is unable to be held effectively in the soil, and it
ends up leaching out into our water courses, causing eutrophication with bacterial and algal
blooms, low oxygen levels and significant environmental detriment to fauna and flora. Besides the
obvious destruction of the natural habitat, it also results in significant national cost to try to sort out
these complex problems.
In the last 100 years, more than 50% of the world’s forests have been destroyed through “slash
and burn agriculture”, to make room for new cropping areas. These newly deforested land areas
produce well in the first two seasons, but they soon fail and the farmers start deforesting new areas
once again, leaving a trail of deforested, decimated lands in their wake. Deforestation results in
carbon emissions, increased temperatures, rapid runoff and reduced humidity levels, having a
major negative effect on localised and global climate that is way beyond just CO2 emissions.
None of the strategies employed by the world’s social or government systems are producing any of
the results required to transform the desperate situations found in poor countries. Instead, poverty
levels are becoming worse and worse.

    2.3. Godly Solutions to the Crisis
In the midst of these negative facts, it is encouraging to know that none of these are
insurmountable. In this Farming God's Way manual we aim to expose the myths of poverty and
unmask the blindness that satan has so deceptively put in place.
The Word of God is full of the promise of restoration. When we, as God’s people, put into practise
the conditions of His Word, exercise obedience, faithfulness and good stewardship, we will not only
inherit those promises in spirit but also in reality.




                                                 5
3. Farming God’s Way

Farming God’s Way is an amazing Godly solution to the food security and poverty crisis facing the
rural poor. Farming God's Way incorporates some of the oldest technologies on the planet, which
were designed by the Creator God before man was even on the face of the earth, when He put His
laws in place to govern His diverse creation, and their complex inter-relations with one another.
        Romans 1:20 “For since the creation of the world His invisible attributes, His eternal power
        and divine nature have been clearly seen, being understood through what has been made,
        so that mankind is without excuse.”
The irrevocable laws of God were established at the very beginning of all time, and have been
clearly displayed to us in creation, and we have no excuse for not seeing or understanding them.
        Gen 2:8,9: “And the Lord God planted a garden toward the east in Eden, and there he
        placed the man whom He had formed. And out of the ground the Lord God caused to grow
        every tree that is pleasing to the sight and good for food; the tree of life also in the midst of
        the garden and the tree of the knowledge of good and evil.”
Genesis 1 clearly establishes the Lord God as the Creator of all, but Genesis 2:8 clearly
establishes that God planted a garden towards the east in Eden. God Himself was the very “First
farmer” and as the creator, He knows what is best for His creation, so He is also the “Master
farmer”.
The garden that God planted in the east of Eden, was the most beautiful, fruitful and productive
food garden that has ever existed. Since the creation of the world, these principles and truths have
been on display for us to learn from, but we have been blinded by leaning on our own
understanding and the traditions of men. The Master Farmer knows what is best for His people,
plants, soils, soil microbiology and how to keep the land in a state of sustainable fruitfulness…all
we need to do is humble ourselves and come closer and closer to aligning our ways with His ways.
In Genesis, we see that God gives man the privilege to rule and reign over the whole earth and all
that it contains. He then gives man his first responsibility.
        Gen 2:15 “The Lord God took the man, and put him into the Garden of Eden to work 5647 it
        and take care8104 of it.”
        5647 ‛âbad - to work; by implication to serve, till, dress, execute, husbandman, keep, labour,
        bring to pass, serve, do service, till, work.
        8104 shâmar - properly to hedge about as with thorns, that is, guard; generally to protect,
        attend to, carefully watch over.
Mankind was mandated by God, to work and carefully look after the garden that He had made. As
the Lord God walked in the garden of Eden with Adam each day, He was discipling him in all areas
of life and work. Can you imagine the depth of wisdom being conveyed by the Creator God to
Adam during those daily discussions?
That first mandate, to work and care for the garden, has not changed since creation. We still have
to measure ourselves against the mandate, with the same application, diligence and faithfulness as
if it were spoken to us today.
God’s master plan was for mankind to live forever in heartfelt relationship with Himself, in
harmonious relationship with one another, in a working relationship with the land which he was
mandated to work and care for.
However, contrary to God’s master plan, Adam and Eve ate from the tree of the knowledge of good
and evil, being overcome by the temptation to become wise like God. The fall of man had dire
consequences.
They were separated from God because of their sin, and His heartfelt relationship with mankind
was broken. The harmonious relationship between Adam and Eve was also broken, being replaced
by blame-shifting and the pointing of the finger. They were then kicked out of the Garden in the
east of Eden and the new ground they were to work was cursed by God. Working the land would
no longer be a joy, it would become hard labour, with a perpetual battle with weeds and thorns.


                                                   6
Mankind could no longer live forever because of the curse of sin and death, so God positioned
cherubim with flaming swords to guard against access to the tree of life, and ensure that no man
could eat from it and live forever in his sinful state.
It is essential to understand that there is no technology in the world that can bring us back into the
fullness of life as it was in the garden in the east of Eden. But God had a plan…
         Colossians 1:19-20 “For it was the Father’s good pleasure for all the fullness to dwell in Him,
         and through Him to reconcile all things to Himself, having made peace through the blood of
         His cross; through Him, I say, whether things on earth or things in heaven.”
It was out of the Father God’s great love for mankind that He sent His only son Jesus to be able to
reconcile all things back to Himself once again. He was sent to re-establish the Master’s plan, by
reconciling man back into heartfelt relationship with the Father God; re-establishing love amongst
all mankind; and to re-establish His redemptive plan for the land. Jesus also restored the Master’s
plan by bridging the flaming sword of the cherubim, allowing us to once again eat from the tree of
life and to have the free gift of eternity with Him. Jesus, who is also called the second Adam,
succeeded where Adam failed.
We need humility of heart to accept Him as Lord and Saviour and to walk in all of His ways. When
we do, He removes the yoke of oppression and sets us free to inherit the fullness of His promised
abundance. What an incredible story of redemption for those who believe!!! No technology or
management can break the curse, only Jesus can. All authority in heaven and on earth has been
given to Him and God’s heartfelt desire is that we would be set free indeed.
It is for this very reason that Farming God’s Way is not just a technology but a well-balanced
Biblical, management and technological solution in the agricultural domain, to equip the poor to
come out of poverty, with what God has put in their hands and to reveal the fullness of Jesus’
promised abundant life.
God has graciously revealed some of these hidden truths, to a wonderful team of Godly men, to
bring “Farming God's Way” to realisation, and yet we humbly acknowledge that we remain in a
continual state of learning from the “Master farmer”.
Man’s ways are in dire contrast to God’s ways. We will continue to fail while leaning on our own
understanding.
         Hos 4:6 “My people perish because of a lack of knowledge.”
The Bible is clear on this fact that it is the lack of knowledge that causes people to perish, not the
lack of capital, inputs, machinery, infrastructure or anything else. Not even hard work can break
the cycle of poverty, if it is done without knowledge. We are fully committed to revealing the
previously hidden truths of creation to the poor, teaching them faithfulness in the agricultural
domain, so that they too can inherit the fullness of Jesus’ promised abundant life.

Boni Story
There is a people group called the Boni, who live in the rainforests on the northern coast of Kenya.
They have lived in absolute simplicity as hunter-gatherers, hunting wild game as their major source
of food for centuries. From the early 2000’s, the Kenyan wildlife authorities have been pressurizing
them to start growing their own food, to reduce the poaching of wildlife in the wilderness areas.
Although this has been a difficult transition for them, it is the slash and burn cropping practices
which have had an even more devastating environmental impact on the coastal forests.




                                                  7
In 2003, a Boni man asked the author the following question. “Why after cutting down and burning
a new portion of forest, would our soils be so fruitful, producing bumper crops in year one; then
declining to an average yield in year two; and then finally be a crop failure in year three; forcing us
to clear, burn and prepare a new portion of the forest for the 4th year?”




          Year 1                                    Year 2                                 Year 3

This is a valid and pertinent question for most agricultural land worldwide, which in its virgin state is
a high producer, but within just three years of man’s intervention, it produces very little.
This drastic yield decline forces the Boni farmers to slash and burn new areas of land, which in
another three year cropping cycle would also be unproductive. This abusive cycle leads to vast
areas of land lying degraded and eroded, taking up to as many as 100 years, in some ecosystems,
for the vegetation to be restored to some measure of its previously glorious state.
The answer is quite simply that man’s ways lead to destruction and God’s ways lead to life in
abundance.
Previously the Boni forest was just like God created it to be - a highly productive rainforest which
was in need of nothing. The forest was capable of succeeding even in drought years and never
slowed or lost its productivity. The trees never lose their leaves or wither and die. They keep on
producing flowers, fruits and seeds and are able to provide food for a vast diversity and number of
animal and bird species.
The Father and Creator put mechanisms in place, which would allow the fauna, flora and soil to co-
exist, with high levels of productivity, no environmental degradation, little or no erosion and a high
buffering ability to withstand all the negative parameters of the harsh African climate.
The Boni slash and burn system does not fulfil God’s mandate “to work and to take care of the
garden”, and neither does the abuse of the land by any small scale or large scale commercial
farmer. Aligning all of our ways to God’s ways is essential for the transformation of communities
and the land. When our gardens are being “worked and cared for” in God’s ways, they will be in a
consistent condition of abundance and improvement, not in a state of crop failure and decline.
What has remained unchanged in that Boni forest before and after man’s intervention?
       Temperature
       Rainfall
       Sunlight hours
       Soil
Practically, what has changed in that forest after man’s intervention? The canopy of trees has been
destroyed. Leaves, twigs, fruits and grasses on the soil surface have been burnt or removed. The
soil structure has been broken down through ploughing. The soil organic matter content has
drastically reduced. The life inside the soil has been destroyed.
If we go to the forests, we can see God’s ways clearly on display. So practically speaking if we had
to be following in God’s ways, has anyone seen God, the Master farmer, ploughing the soil He


                                                8
created? Or has anyone seen God destroying the mulch covers through burning, removal or
incorporating it mechanically?
The answer is quite obviously no. God neither ploughs the soil nor destroys the beautiful blanket of
mulch cover, which we refer to as God’s Blanket. So why do we ‘lean on our own understanding’
and not trust in the way He has shown to us since the beginning? Do we presume to have a better
understanding than God? Or is it our pride to do it our way? Or is it that we still trust in our earthly
father’s ways and traditions?
Physically, the laws that God has put in place in creation for the most productive ecosystems in the
world include:

   1. little or no soil disturbance;
   2. a thick blanket cover on the surface of the ground;
   3. a great biodiversity of species;
   4. where plants have been designed to feed from the top;
   5. each with their own distinctive canopy cover;
   6. and God is always constant - He never changes His ways.

These are some of the laws which allow for the fruitful functioning of His creation. The Master
Farmer knows what’s best, and we should be doing what we have been shown by our Father,
following what we see Him do in creation as closely as we can, whilst emulating His laws in a
manageable agricultural way, where there are high demands and pressures.


     The 6 Technology keys are:
     1. No Ploughing;
     2. 100% Cover with God’s Blanket that is 2.5cm thick;
     3. Practicing Biodiversity Through Rotations and other Methods;
     4. Feeding Plants from the Top;
     5. Ensuring Canopy Cover;
     6. Permanence.


The spiritual reasons for this failing agricultural system are less obvious and yet these are even
more crucial to gain an understanding of. God sees and knows all things, the condition of our
hearts, our actions, our thoughts and our motives. It is impossible to conceal anything from Him
and He is not mocked. Read Hosea 2.
         Hosea 2:8 “You did not know that it was I …”
God’s people had been prostituting themselves with the idols and gods of the Canaanites who
were there before them, just like Hosea’s wife. They had ‘turned after their lovers’ and ‘acted
shamefully’, acknowledging them as the supplier of bread, water, wool, flax, oil, wine, silver and
gold. So the Lord hedged up Israel’s way with thorns, and built a wall so that she could not find her
way. Because of this idolatry and the acknowledgement of other gods, He declares ‘I will take back
My grain at harvest time, new wine, wool, flax, destroy her vines and fig trees’. All of these
judgements were so that God would humble His people to come back to Himself and acknowledge
Him, so that it may go well with them and they may be blessed. His judgements are all rooted in
His amazing, deep, faithful love for His people. God’s goodness is demonstrated by the fact that
He disciplines those that He loves.



                                                9
        The 6 Biblical Keys are:
         1. Acknowledge God and God alone;
         2. Consider Your Ways for You are the Temple of the Living God;
         3. Understanding Gods All Sufficiency;
         4. What you Sow you will Reap;
         5. Bring the Tithes and Offerings Back to God;
         6. Stake your Claim


Alongside the many technological and spiritual failings amongst farmers, there are management
principles that need adjustment as well.



         The 4 Management Keys are:
          Doing Things ….
          1. On Time;
          2. To High Standards;
          3. With Minimal Wastage;
          4. With the Fruit of the Spirit.



All three of these spheres of influence - Biblical, Management and Technology - are interwoven
throughout this Farming God's Way material, to be able to equip you to walk in the fullness of
God’s ways in the agricultural domain.

Farming God’s Way is a holistic model of agriculture, meeting small scale farmers where they are
at, whilst dealing astoundingly well with the negative parameters facing them. Countless success
stories abound from around the world, where farmers have implemented Farming God's Way
wholeheartedly, where lives and livelihoods have been transformed, with the evidence of food
security, radically increased yields, profitability and the restoration of farmers’ self-worth.




                                             10
4. History of Farming God's Way

It is often asked where Farming God's Way began and who pioneered this, but the reality is this is
the very oldest technology on the planet and we are simply trying to follow the Master Farmer as
closely as possible in a manageable agricultural environment. The first man to practise Farming
God's Way was actually Adam, when God placed him in the garden to work it and take care of it.
In recent years there have been many elements of the technology which have been pioneered by
leading agronomists, and just a few of them will be mentioned to give them the respect that they
deserve for reshaping the agricultural domain by breaking away from the conventional practises of
their time.

No Ploughing
In July 1943 Edward Faulkner’s 1st Edition of his book “Plowman’s Folly” was published. This book
was like hitting a hornet’s nest on a hot summer’s day. The book caused an outrage in the farming
community, for both going completely against the grain of the “deep ploughing trend”, but also for
implying that it was a foolish thing to plough in the very first place. The Times magazine called it
“one of the most revolutionary ideas in agriculture history”, and it certainly was the biggest
challenge to the agricultural status quo in the 20th century. In that era, it was incomprehensible to
consider farming without ploughing. Ploughing had been a tradition passed down through the ages,
and taught as being the only way to grow crops, from school level right through to higher education
institutions. When Faulkner started his trials in 1937, the US Department of Agriculture laughed his
theories off, and even when he showcased his trials to them, they rubbished them as improper. But
the murmurings of the ailing agri sector, along with impoverished and eroding soils, created a
tipping point, which eventually gave enough momentum for farmers to start making the drastic
changes required.
However, it was only in the 1990’s that the no-till movement really started gathering significant
momentum, with over 205 million hectares planted without ploughing in 2021 worldwide. [19]
The largest implementers of no-till are the USA, followed closely by Brazil. The South American
countries of Argentina, Brazil and Paraguay have had the most rapid uptake, with over 68% of their
cropped land areas planted without ploughing. The South American farmers made these changes,
not because of environmental or stewardship reasons but rather the economics thereof including
increased crop yields; reduced costs of labour, machinery, pesticides, herbicides and fertiliser; and
reduced risks.
Leading agronomists and no-till farmers came to Africa in the early 1970’s to equip the
departments of agriculture with their new found success with “no ploughing” and for them to see if
it would work across different soils and bioclimatic regions. However, Africa has been very slow to
adopt and change, and by 2022 only had around 2 million hectares which are farmed without
ploughing, most of which are in South Africa.
It is nevertheless a great tragedy that the vast majority of no-till farmers are dependent on
Roundup/Glyphosate herbicides and planting Genetically Modified seed cultivars and have traded
one environmental problem for another.

Mulching
In the early 1970’s a lady by the name of Ruth Stout started doing experimentation, planting
vegetables with no ploughing and using mulch. She was nicknamed “Mrs Mulch” because of her
deep conviction that a very heavy mulch cover was enough to both feed her plants and fulfil the
necessary soil remediation required to grow healthy vegetables without ploughing. In one of her
books on the subject called “Gardening without work”, she says “you call me Mrs Mulch, but I didn’t
create it God did”.
Since those early pioneering days, significant advancements have been made to mulch large fields
using cover crops and double cropping techniques of small and large staple grains. Worldwide
there has been a breakthrough in understanding that soils must not be left naked for any length of
time.


                                             11
As mankind strives to go “green”, many advances in the agricultural sector have come about with
Minimal till, Zero till, Conservation farming and Regenerative farming techniques. However, how
many of these actually give the Creator God His rightful place as the First and Master Farmer, or
the credit for what has been clearly on display through His glorious creation?

African Changes Birthed in a Crisis
In October 1982, Brian Oldreive became the manager of Hinton Estate, near Bindura in the north
east of Zimbabwe. Over the next 2 years he anxiously noticed that they were experiencing
increasing land preparation costs, fertility and yield declines, water runoff losses, horrendous soil
erosion, and the costs of inputs were increasing annually. Hinton Estate was in a precarious
financial situation and had not made a profit for many years.
In his desperation and concern that the farm was on its way to bankruptcy, he sought the Lord’s
counsel. Often large scale farmers overlook the fact that the very roots of Farming God’s Way were
developed in a large scale commercial farming crisis, and thereafter adapted for the small scale
farmers, so that they too could benefit from the outstanding results obtained.
Brian asked God to teach him how to overcome the difficulties he was encountering and that’s
when God began to speak to him about how He manages creation, where He never ploughs or
inverts the soil; and where He never destroys or buries the decaying plant material on the surface
of the ground. God also spoke clearly of the stewardship and excellence farmers are called to
adopt, by caring for and managing the land in a way that would ensure that farmlands could be
passed on as an inheritance from one generation to another and bring glory to God.

Start Small and be Faithful with Little
In the early 1970’s some US based agronomists had approached and taught the Zimbabwean
agricultural authorities about what they called “Conservation Farming techniques”, but due to the
department’s half-hearted attempts, they had failed. When Brian Oldreive approached them with
his ideas, they told him they had tried it and it would never ever work. He decided that if he did
what the Lord had shown him, that it was extremely important to do a small trial plot of 2 hectares
to a very high standard, that left no chance for poor management to be the potential cause of
failure.
         1 Corinthians 10:31 ”… whatever you do, do all to the glory of God.”
The first 2 hectares were a radical success using simple hand hoes. Brian then increased Hinton’s
Farming God's Way area annually, with their experience growing until the whole farm was
converted over a 5 year time period.
Year 1 - Land area 2 ha
Year 2 - Land area 10 ha
Year 3 - Land area 50 ha
Year 4 - Land area 250 ha
Year 5 – entire cropped area 1,000 ha
The total cropped area of 1,000 ha was made up of 675 ha in summer (maize, seed-maize and
soyabeans), and 325 ha in winter (winter wheat).
Brian’s aim to implement 2 ha out of a total of 1,000 ha seems too insignificant to become
consequential. However, it is upon those first 2 ha standards, that thousands of farmers have been
trained today. A seemingly insignificant trial plot became the starting point for the transformation of
the continent’s agricultural domain.
         Zechariah 4:10 “Do not despise these small beginnings, for the Lord rejoices to see the work
         begin…”
Although this text talks of a mere foundation laid by Zerubbabel, it is the excitement and rejoicing
of the Lord that is most interesting. This is God’s way. He starts with what we have in our hand and
establishes character, faithfulness and stewardship values in us and when we master these He
then can put us in charge of bigger things.


                                                12
Starting small is a difficult and seemingly insignificant way to start, but it bears the most lasting
fruit. We are encouraged not to despise these small beginnings but to exercise faithfulness with
them.

Use what God has put in your Hand
Brian is a man of the highest calibre, yet he is also an extremely humble and God-fearing man.
When the Lord had revealed His truths about the technology, they had no machinery to do
anything like what they saw God do in creation. Instead of giving up, they worked with what God
had put in their hands: hoes. At their peak manual planting stage, they were farming 650 ha of
cropped land area with 1,100 labourers at planting time. This was an incredible commitment and
required a lot of faith, but they persevered and succeeded. Later on, with the farm making such
incredible profits, they were able to purchase imported large scale machinery such as no-till drills.
However, Brian still testifies to the highest yields ever recorded as being under a hand planted
system, rather than the machine planted technique, because of the precision in seed placement
and covering operations.

Believing in the African People
At that point in history in Africa, there was very little room given to black management on farms,
due to the colonial influences of white supremacy. Black Africans were never given any
opportunities for training and development. Brian Oldreive had two white managers under him
when he arrived and both gave him so much trouble that he decided not to take on any other white
management. When he told the Hinton estate board members, they were shocked that he would
consider raising up black managers, but he eventually persuaded them to let him try. Once given
the responsibility, training and mentoring, these managers excelled in their new found measures of
management and never looked back. After the collapse of the agriculture economy of Zimbabwe
from 2002-2008, these very same managers were able to continue producing profits for their new
farm owners. This is testament to the true discipleship of the managers: that they would show
lasting fruit in the absence of their mentors.
This is such an important part of the Farming God's Way outlook. We believe that all peoples,
tribes and tongues can farm just as well as anyone else, given the opportunity, education and
mentoring. We must have faith in empowering all people groups, just as the Lord does, that they
can realise their own God-given potential. The love we have for each other is a love given by God
for our new found family in Him, a love which shows no discrimination against colour or race or
gender or hierarchical status.

The Pride of Men
It is interesting that the neighbouring farmers, whose farms were in the same steady state of
decline in profitability as Hinton Estate had been, refused to listen to Brian’s encouragement to
change to doing things God’s way. They continued to ‘lean on their own understanding’ and to walk
in the way of their fathers, with their pride standing between them and a successful transition.
Many of these farmers ended up going bankrupt and their farms were lost. It is this very same
pride that is the most limiting of all factors to change, both for commercial and small scale farmers
alike.

Faithfulness Rewarded
Meanwhile on Hinton Estate, crop yields and profitability improved dramatically, allowing them to
buy up some of these adjoining farms going through liquidation. The estate increased from 1,000
ha to 3,500 ha, with most of the area being double cropped, in rotations, for the 18 year duration of
Brian’s management. A vast array of crops including maize, wheat, soyabeans, groundnuts, cotton,
sorghum, sunflowers, wheat, sugar cane, coffee, vegetables, pecan nuts and macadamia nuts
were grown successfully on the farm. As a result of the successes, Hinton Estate received several
achievement awards including: maize grower of the year, wheat grower of the year, as well as
being a member of the elite Ten Ton Club for maize yields in excess of 10 tons/ha (best 13,9 t/ha).
Brian was also invited to speak internationally at conservation tillage conferences.



                                               13
The Plight of the Poor
The wonderful successes on Hinton Estate stood out in dire contrast to the poverty of the adjoining
communal farmers’ fields, who were practicing conventional farming. They suffered from poor
yields and crop failures during dry seasons, with severe sheet and gully erosion being the norm.
God began to stir a deep desire in Brian’s heart to transfer Farming God’s Way practises to these
communal farmers. In the first year, they taught the technology to the neighbouring community
using an individual farmer’s field as a model. They prepared, fertilised and planted a hectare of
land, but at harvest time, they came to find a total crop failure with the maize completely overtaken
by weeds. They were shocked but decided to try again, but in the 2nd year the very same thing
happened. Investigations showed that no weeding had been done at all and the maize crop had
been totally out competed by the jungle of weeds. The reason for no weeding was that the
community had become jealous of this farmer receiving all this attention and assistance, so they
paid a witchdoctor to put juju (magic potion) on the field. Overcome by fear, this family had been
too terrified to even put a foot in their own field. This was such a revelation of just how strong the
curse of jealousy and witchcraft is amongst those living in animism.
Many years later, 58 one hectare trial plots were put in across the whole nation of Zimbabwe, using
hybrid seed and inorganic fertiliser, covering every bioclimatic group and region. Thousands of
people were trained in the technology through this initiative and yet despite the successes of the 1
ha plots, the method of training strangely met with minimal implementation from the poor. It is
important to note how important the Biblical and Management keys are in breaking the yoke of
poverty…but these were only added much later.
After handing the management of Hinton Estate over to the widowed owner’s son, Brian
established a large commercial farm, Agriway Community Church and Farm, with the purpose of
training the poor. Small scale farmers were allocated 1 ha, 2 ha and 5 ha plots depending on their
level of expertise and faithfulness. Unfortunately, during the Zimbabwean land grab Agriway was
seized by the government, along with many commercial farms throughout the nation, and the
Oldreives were physically forced off their farm and they lost everything.

Extension - Early Zambian Influence
In 1996, the Zambian authorities were so interested in this new technology that they combined with
the World Bank to roll it out at large scale. The World Bank were keenly interested due to the
commitment by the agricultural authorities as well as the significant reduction in financial risks. The
Zambian agricultural extension department were taught by Brian Oldreive, and they in turn took it
throughout the nation with significant results. However, the Zambians soon changed important
fundamentals of the technology, until it could really not be said to be from the original blueprint at
all. There was no incorporation of the holistic teaching of Farming God's Way and only certain
elements of the technology were rolled out, which was a great tragedy, considering the potential to
extend God’s Kingdom.

Operation Joseph and Handouts
Operation Joseph was launched in 2000 and by 2007 they were reaching 2,700 families in all 8
provinces of Zimbabwe. This was an ongoing and successful outreach supported by the Food and
Agricultural Organisation (FAO), that distributed inputs and training to each family for 1 ha of crops
to help feed those families and create models for others to follow. The Operation Joseph team,
ably led by Alan Norton, was able to touch thousands of lives, with amazing testimonies of
provision especially during the drought and famine years of the early 2000s. The vision was for
farmers to eventually sell back their produce, and then a portion of their income would be used to
pay for their inputs in the next season. However, the plan was all established on an
incentive/handout based system where the farmers would get the inputs only if they followed the
technology. The plan was to start with a handout of both seed and inorganic fertiliser, thereafter to
wean the communities off the inorganic fertiliser handout in the 2nd year, and then off the seed in
the following year. Sadly, the strategy of gradually reduced incentives never actually materialised
as the poor were only embracing the technology because of the incentives/handouts and not
because of a wholehearted adoption of the technology. After 8 years, the Operation Joseph



                                              14
program was shut down as the farmers were so locked into the dependency cycle and were not
prepared to continue without being given the handouts. [12]
The 3rd Biblical Key of Farming God's Way is “Understanding God’s All Sufficiency” which
essentially opens up the eyes of farmers, to see what God has put in their hands, and put it to
good use so they can break the dependency cycle. In Farming God's Way, we heartily discourage
the use of incentives, handouts, handups or cash loans, as these just put a yoke on the necks of
the poor. Isaiah 58 mandates us to be those that loosen, remove and break every yoke to set the
poor free - not to bind them into dependency cycles.

The Shift from Inorganic to Organic Inputs
In the early years of extension, the technology was all taught coming off the commercial farm
Hinton Estate, and advocated the use of inorganic fertiliser and agricultural lime as the main forms
of fertilisation and pH control, with organic inputs side lined as weaker alternatives. However, from
2003 onwards, the Farming God's Way team promoted a shift from inorganic purchased or
handout inputs, to the preferred organic inputs like manure, compost, anthill soil and woodash
amongst others. These organic inputs proved themselves to be very successful mainline inputs for
everyone, that are readily available to “use the resources that God has put in our hands”.

The Biblical Keys
In 2003, during a Farming God's Way mission into Malawi, the author wrote the 6 Biblical keys,
which were missing ingredients of the resource suite of Farming God's Way. The Biblical keys are
very specifically geared to the agricultural domain and bring the Word of God into everyday farm
life in real tangible ways. These Biblical keys have become the foundation stones for Farming
God's Way to have a successful breakthrough in farmer’s lives and livelihoods. Farming
communities are implementing Farming God's Way first and foremost because of their change of
heart to align their ways to God’s ways. They are not changing their ways because they are given
incentives, handouts or handups or chasing profits. We believe that out of the transformation of the
heart, through Jesus, comes the renewing of the mind in good management, which in turn results
in the redemptive purpose of God being revealed through the work of our hands in our farmlands.

Foundations for Farming
In 2009, the Zimbabwean forum, under the leadership of Brian Oldreive, established a non-profit
organisation called “Foundations for Farming” (FfF) under their church covering of New Frontiers
International. Their vision was to be able to be more amenable to non-christian sectors such as
government agencies and main line NGO’s. FfF thereafter developed their own set of materials
and values. Although Farming God's Way and FfF have similar roots, they are distinctly different
entities with different leadership, different core values, different resources and different extension
methodologies, amongst others.

Farming God's Way Stewardship Team
In 2009, the Stewardship Team of Farming God's Way was formulated to guide and steward the
work of Farming God's Way going forward. The Stewardship Team was made up of August
Basson, Carl Schmidt, Grant Dryden and Wilhelm Els, and were responsible for establishing the
“Values of Farming God's Way”. These values have been essential for the heart, relationships,
extension and strategic build into the nations. In 2022, the standing Stewardship Team members
were Carl Schmidt, Chris Sperling, Dan Wiens, Dickson Shuwali and Grant Dryden.

Resources
The author wrote the 1st Farming God's Way manual called the Field Guide in 2003, to capture the
fullness of the 3 spheres of Farming God's Way in Biblical, Management and Technology keys.
In 2008, the 1st Edition of the Trainer's Reference Guide was written by the author.
During 2008 and 2009, the Farming God's Way Main Video Series was developed by the Bountiful
Grains Trust and Rooftop Productions, with the 18 part series covering all the Biblical,
Management and Technology keys.


                                              15
After many years of research and experimentation, the author wrote the 1st Edition of the
Vegetable Guide in 2013, and the subsequent 2nd Edition of the Vegetable Guide was released in
2017.
In 2018, the Vegetable Video Series was developed by the Bountiful Grains Trust with Rooftop
Productions, and is made up of 32 videos covering the Vegetable Guide contents.
In 2020, the Gardens of Faithfulness program was developed by the Bountiful Grains Trust team
and added a new dimension to the home nutrition garden strategy.
Currently the video series on flash drive includes the Main Series, Vegetable Series and Gardens
of Faithfulness videos.
The programming of the new material has been designed in such a way to allow facilitators to take
delegates through all the Farming God's Way material over six 3 hour sessions.
There is also a 28 module Video series available to Bible Schools and colleges, with the Farming
God's Way material broken down into 30 minute time slots which is convenient for integration into
curriculum placement.

Resource Translations
We have always had a culture of “developing resources that will outlive and outrace us” particularly
with the development of translations into local languages, to make the information readily available
to people from all tribes and tongues. The translations have been developed in partnership with
many individuals and organisations around the world, who have helped to make them a reality. In
2022, the Trainer's Reference Guide was in English, Chichewa, Zulu, Xhosa, Portuguese, Swahili,
French and Spanish. The Field Guide was in 16 languages; the Vegetable Guide was in 7
languages; and the video series was in 9 languages including English, Xhosa, Zulu, Sesotho,
Chichewa, Portuguese, Swahili, French and Bambarra.

Making Real History
The best Farming God's Way history story to tell is of Dickson Shuwali in Malawi. His father was a
farmer in the Mapelera district, with 2 wives and many children. Dickson’s family were very poor,
and he would often go hungry or eat a meal every 2nd day. In 2001, when Dickson was just a
teenage boy, he was asked to translate for the author during a two week relief mission. It was
during this incredible time of ministry and service to the poorest of the poor, where they developed
a life-long friendship. In 2003, he once again was called on to be the translator of the Farming
God's Way seminars where he learned of Farming God's Way first hand.
Dickson was then sent to a year of Bible school in Jeffreys Bay in South Africa, which was a
wonderful experience where he learned so much about the Word and the things of the Spirit.
However, it was the transition of going back home again, from the city’s wealth and amenities, to a
desperate level of poverty with no opportunities or hope for the future, that was nearly unbearable
for him. When he was at his very lowest low in his life, the Lord reminded him of Farming God's
Way, and he decided to give it a try as he had nothing left to lose.
He started with the 1 acre plot his father had left him as an inheritance, which was in a terrible
state, yielding an average of just three 50kg bags of maize in a season. The soil was so
impoverished, eroded and rock hard, that you could barely break the surface. The Lower Shire
area of Malawi is extremely hot with daytime temperatures reaching 45ºC each day in summer and
35ºC in winter, with extremely erratic rainfall and frequent droughts.
In Dickson’s 1st year of implementing Farming God's Way he didn’t plough or make ridges. He
used only woodash and manure in planting stations and planted his maize, harvesting an
incredible 5 bags in a national drought, when his neighbours harvested nothing. It should be noted
that this had never been done in the region before. Farmers would traditionally plough by hand and
work their soils into 45cm tall ridges and plant on these ridges. So not only was this “flat planting”
but also “not ploughing”, and using what many would call “rubbish” (God’s Blanket) on the ground
surface. These were techniques that were completely foreign to the farming community and
brought lots of sniggers and snide remarks…right up until harvest time that is, when everyone
stood amazed. In his 2nd year he carried in sufficient blanket material to cover his whole field (more


                                              16
on this later), and repeated the same Farming God's Way steps, harvesting 45 bags. In his 3rd year
he harvested 54 bags and in his 4th year he harvested 69 bags, all from the very same tract of
ground. That is an incredible progression, all based on faithful stewardship where he was able to
increase his yields by 23 times in just 4 years. Over the subsequent years his yields have always
been well over 40 bags in drought years and over 60 bags in years of good rainfall. Even in years
where there has been widespread flooding of the Lower Shire floodplains, when his fields have
been literally 2 feet under water and his neighbour’s crops have gone yellow and died, his have
come through miraculously. God is good all the time and will help us through the extreme climatic
cycles of droughts and floods.
Dickson has been able to make sustainable profits over the years which have empowered him to
increase their family’s wealth incredibly well. They were able to buy another winter bean field first
and many years later a winter irrigation field. Farming God’s Way has allowed Dickson to be able
to put his children into good schools and walk in God’s blessing as a family. God’s heart is that the
poor would not stay in poverty but come out from under the yoke of oppression and Dickson and
his family have been able to do that through farming and using what God has put in their hands.
However, it is not the excellent harvests, profitability or soil improvements in Dickson Shuwali’s
story that should excite us; it is his influence into the nation of Malawi and the example he has set
for the nations of the world, through self-sacrifice and servanthood, to the poor all around him.
Dickson travels and teaches the poor in villages far and wide, carrying the life-giving message of
the gospel of Jesus Christ and Farming God's Way wherever he goes. He has taught tens of
thousands of farmers and has thousands of farmers on board with him. He has discipled many,
many, trainers who are all teaching through their church networks or NGO’s and is more than
happy to share his time and skills to establish others as influencers in their own right. He is most
passionate about seeing the widows and elderly never go hungry and patiently mentors them into
God’s all sufficiency. He will walk farm fields for days on end to ensure that everyone has a visit,
some discipleship and a prayer at their fields. He is dedicated to the poor and never gives up on
them. He has turned down high profile jobs and positions to carry on the work of transforming the
lives of the poor through the impartation of Farming God's Way skills. This is what Farming God's
Way is all about and we need thousands of Dicksons!

Current Influence
This Farming God's Way resource has been embraced and used successfully by thousands of
churches, missional organisations, NGO’s and individuals all around the world.
In 2022 there were 63 “Accredited trainers” who were working in different organisations and
churches; 181 mentees in the process of accreditation; along with hundreds of wholehearted non-
accredited trainers, successfully extending Farming God's Way through their own spheres of
influence. This is history in the making, as you learn, get involved and add to the collective
momentum of this family of believers, who love God and who are fully persuaded to make a
difference amongst the poor, with this wonderful resource that God has entrusted to us.
Social media platforms:
Farming God's Way website www.farming-gods-way.org
YouTube channel https://www.youtube.com/channel/UCkRvml2KKMpstMwrfgKCVyw
Facebook Page - Farming God's Way
Facebook Groups - for many different regions
All of these account for hundreds of thousands of points of contact with people all over the world,
which is simply impossible to measure. We are expectant and excited for the Lord to bring about
exponential growth in the future, to see more and more farmers’ lives transformed through the
gospel of the Kingdom of God in the agricultural domain.




                                             17
5. Motivation

It is not by chance that God has called you; however, it is a weighty responsibility to walk in this
calling. Higher learning is an incredible privilege, which is given by God to those whom He sees fit
to be implementers and communicators of this message of hope. You have an added responsibility
to be a faithful steward and communicator of this message, so that others can benefit from your
understanding.
         Luke 12:48b “From everyone who has been given much, much will be required; and to
         whom they entrusted much, of him they will ask all the more.”
God has given you this privilege that you would become a good leader, teacher and shepherd.
Unfortunately, positions of authority are generally used for selfish gain and to attain positions of
power.
         James 3:1 “Let not many of you become teachers, my brethren, knowing that as such we
         will incur a stricter judgment.”
James cautions every teacher, that to whom much is given, much is required and that you will be
judged more severely than others because of the trust and favour that God has shown to you. The
knowledge you gain is not intended for your selfish gain, nor for you to be given places of honour
by men, but rather that you become a vessel that can usher in His Kingdom through your acts of
service.
The judgement of the selfish and power hungry shepherds (those entrusted with positions of
leadership over people’s lives) is clearly laid out through scripture.
         Jeremiah 23:1-4 "Woe to the shepherds who are destroying and scattering the sheep of My
         pasture!" declares the Lord. Therefore thus says the Lord God of Israel concerning the
         shepherds who are tending My people: "You have scattered My flock and driven them away,
         and have not attended to them; behold, I am about to attend to you for the evil of your
         deeds," declares the Lord. "Then I Myself will gather the remnant of My flock out of all the
         countries where I have driven them and bring them back to their pasture, and they will be
         fruitful and multiply. I will also raise up shepherds over them and they will tend them; and
         they will not be afraid any longer, nor be terrified, nor will any be missing," declares the
         Lord.”
         Ezekiel 34:1-19 “Then the word of the Lord came to me saying, Son of man, prophesy
         against the shepherds of Israel. Prophesy and say to those shepherds, 'Thus says the Lord
         God, "Woe, shepherds of Israel who have been feeding themselves! Should not the
         shepherds feed the flock? You eat the fat and clothe yourselves with the wool, you slaughter
         the fat sheep without feeding the flock. Those who are sickly you have not strengthened, the
         diseased you have not healed, the broken you have not bound up, the scattered you have
         not brought back, nor have you sought for the lost; but with force and with severity you have
         dominated them. They were scattered for lack of a shepherd, and they became food for
         every beast of the field and were scattered. My flock wandered through all the mountains
         and on every high hill; My flock was scattered over all the surface of the earth, and there was
         no one to search or seek for them. Therefore, you shepherds, hear the word of the Lord: "As
         I live," declares the Lord God, "surely because My flock has become a prey, My flock has
         even become food for all the beasts of the field for lack of a shepherd, and My shepherds did
         not search for My flock, but rather the shepherds fed themselves and did not feed My flock;
         therefore, you shepherds, hear the word of the Lord: Thus says the Lord God, "Behold, I am
         against the shepherds, and I will demand My sheep from them and make them cease from
         feeding sheep. So the shepherds will not feed themselves anymore, but I will deliver My flock
         from their mouth, so that they will not be food for them."
         For thus says the Lord God, "Behold, I Myself will search for My sheep and seek them out.
         As a shepherd cares for his herd in the day when he is among his scattered sheep, so I will
         care for My sheep and will deliver them from all the places to which they were scattered on a
         cloudy and gloomy day. I will bring them out from the peoples and gather them from the
         countries and bring them to their own land; and I will feed them on the mountains of Israel,
         by the streams, and in all the inhabited places of the land. I will feed them in a good pasture,
         and their grazing ground will be on the mountain heights of Israel. There they will lie down on



                                                 18
         good grazing ground and feed in rich pasture on the mountains of Israel. I will feed My flock
         and I will lead them to rest," declares the Lord God. I will seek the lost, bring back the
         scattered, bind up the broken and strengthen the sick; but the fat and the strong I will
         destroy. I will feed them with judgment. As for you, My flock, thus says the Lord God,
         'Behold, I will judge between one sheep and another, between the rams and the male goats.
         Is it too slight a thing for you that you should feed in the good pasture, that you must tread
         down with your feet the rest of your pastures? Or that you should drink of the clear waters,
         that you must foul the rest with your feet? As for My flock, they must eat what you tread
         down with your feet and drink what you foul with your feet!'"
         Matthew 23:2-6: "The scribes and the Pharisees have seated themselves in the chair of
         Moses; therefore, all that they tell you, do and observe, but do not do according to their
         deeds; for they say things and do not do them. They tie up heavy burdens and lay them on
         men's shoulders, but they themselves are unwilling to move them with so much as a finger.
         But they do all their deeds to be noticed by men; for they broaden their phylacteries and
         lengthen the tassels of their garments. They love the place of honor at banquets and the
         chief seats in the synagogues,”
In direct contrast, Jesus demonstrated and instructed us to walk in servant leadership. He spent
His life fulfilling His Father’s desire for Him, never elevating Himself, but rather humbling Himself
and exercising unselfishness.
         Matthew 23:11 "But the greatest among you shall be your servant.”
         Matthew 23:12 "Whoever exalts himself shall be humbled; and whoever humbles himself
         shall be exalted.”
Jesus’ fulfilled the role of the good Shepherd. He has demonstrated this so that you will follow Him
as a true disciple of His, walking in humility, unselfishness and the fear of God.
         John 13:14,15 "If I then, the Lord and the Teacher, washed your feet, you also ought to wash
         one another's feet. For I gave you an example that you also should do as I did to you.”
In the vast majority of poor communities, church leaders are ministering to sheep that are farmers,
and yet very few of them show consideration for the fact that their congregants need knowledge
and skills in the area of agriculture.
A wonderful testimony of a good shepherd, is Bishop Charles Mithowa, of the Kingfisher movement
of churches in the Goliat district of Malawi. He began doing Farming God's Way to improve his own
family’s food security situation. After having proven its benefits himself, he voluntarily began to
actively share about Farming God's Way through their movement of churches. They then set up
the “Heaven’s Family Love Groups”, to disciple and encourage one another in the journey. The
work continued to expand with more and more farmers getting on board with astounding success
and adoption rates. Very soon Bishop Mithowa started both advocating and teaching Farming
God's Way through his influence at the regional pastors fraternal. He took them on tours to learn
from their model farms, and these leaders in turn adopted and started teaching Farming God's
Way in their churches and communities, and the fire has continued to spread in leaps and bounds
from there. This is such a great testimony of what happens when leaders simply humble
themselves, appropriate this knowledge, and empower their communities with the influence that
God has entrusted to them.
         James 4:10 “Humble yourselves in the presence of the Lord, and He will exalt you.”
From the outset of this material, take a humble view of yourself and follow Jesus’ example of
servant leadership, considering others as more important than yourself. Jesus modelled on earth a
heart of compassion and love for the poor, which we in turn should display in our lives.
         Luke 4:18,19 “The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he has anointed me to proclaim
         good news to the poor. He has sent me to proclaim liberty to the captives and recovery of
         sight to the blind, to set at liberty those who are oppressed, and to proclaim the year of the
         Lord’s favour.”




                                                  19
The Word of God warns us not to overlook the poor and is full of wonderful promises for those who
consider the poor.
         Proverbs 21:13 “Whoever closes his ear to the cry of the poor will himself call out and not be
         answered.”
         Proverbs 28:27 “Whoever gives to the poor will not want, but he who hides his eyes will get
         many a curse.”
         Psalm 41:1 “Blessed is the one who considers the poor! In the day of trouble the Lord
         delivers him”
         Proverbs 19:17 “Whoever is generous to the poor lends to the Lord, and He will repay him
         for his deed”
Let us ensure that our motivation is out of a deep, heartfelt consideration for the poor. As those
who have been entrusted with much by the Lord, God expects much from us, not for our own
selfish ambitions, but that it would be an expression of Jesus’ modelled humility to serve the
poorest of the poor. There is no greater work than this.
Farming God's Way Motto: Our extension of Farming God's Way should be rooted in compassion,
motivated by obedience and delivered with much love.




                                                 20
6. The True Fast

The founding scripture of Farming God's Way is Isaiah 58.
        Isaiah 58:1-12 "Cry loudly, do not hold back; Raise your voice like a trumpet, and declare to
        My people their transgression and to the house of Jacob their sins. Yet they seek Me day by
        day and delight to know My ways, as a nation that has done righteousness and has not
        forsaken the ordinance of their God. They ask Me for just decisions, they delight in the
        nearness of God.
        Why have we fasted and You do not see? Why have we humbled ourselves and You do not
        notice?
        Behold, on the day of your fast you find your desire, and drive hard all your workers. Behold,
        you fast for contention and strife and to strike with a wicked fist. You do not fast like you do
        today to make your voice heard on high. Is it a fast like this which I choose, a day for a man
        to humble himself? Is it for bowing one's head like a reed and for spreading out sackcloth
        and ashes as a bed? Will you call this a fast, even an acceptable day to the Lord?
        Is this not the fast which I choose, to loosen the bonds of wickedness, to undo the bands of
        the yoke, and to let the oppressed go free and break every yoke? Is it not to divide your
        bread with the hungry and bring the homeless poor into the house; when you see the naked,
        to cover him; and not to hide yourself from your own flesh?
        Then your light will break out like the dawn, and your recovery will speedily spring forth; and
        your righteousness will go before you; the glory of the Lord will be your rear guard. Then you
        will call, and the Lord will answer; you will cry, and He will say, 'Here I am.' If you remove the
        yoke from your midst, the pointing of the finger and speaking wickedness, and if you give
        yourself to the hungry and satisfy the desire of the afflicted, then your light will rise in
        darkness and your gloom will become like midday. The Lord will continually guide you, and
        satisfy your desire in scorched places, and give strength to your bones; and you will be like a
        well watered garden, and like a spring of water whose waters do not fail. Those from among
        you will rebuild the ancient ruins; you will raise up the age-old foundations; and you will be
        called the repairer of the breach, the restorer of the streets in which to dwell.”
Isaiah 58 begins with a stern rebuke for God’s people, as termed by the House of Jacob and My
people. This rebuke is not aimed at the ungodly, but at believers, those who are called by His
name.
God exposes their hypocrisy in their daily seeking after Him, doing righteous acts, not forsaking His
ordinances, asking for just decisions, delighting in being in His presence, humbling themselves,
fasting, but doing all these things with selfish intent and having no consideration for others.
        1 Samuel 15:22 “Samuel said, ‘Has the Lord as much delight in burnt offerings and sacrifices
        as in obeying the voice of the Lord? Behold, to obey is better than sacrifice, and to heed than
        the fat of rams.’”
God wants us to obey His weightier provisions of the law – justice, mercy and faithfulness
(Matthew 23:23) and desires to see us incorporate these values into our everyday life and outlook.
He is able to see through all our actions, motives and thoughts, and when He encounters
selfishness and pride He resists us, closes His ears and removes His glory. Many Christians feel
that under the new covenant they are exempt from passages of scripture that bring the rebuke of
self-righteousness and selfishness such as Isaiah 58. However, Jesus modelled for us to walk in
unselfishness and humility when He came to earth, taking on the form of a man, being raised as a
son by an earthly father and working as a carpenter. When He began His ministry he continued in
humility, ministering to the poor, broken, sick, oppressed and demon possessed.
        Matthew 16:24-25 “Then Jesus said to His disciples, “If anyone wants to come after Me, he
        must deny himself, take up his cross, and follow Me. For whoever wants to save his life will
        lose it; but whoever loses his life for My sake will find it.”
In His extension, He said to His disciples “follow Me”, teaching them by example what they should
in turn do. All records of His interaction with wealthy or prominent people were when they


                                                 21
approached Him, while He focused on pouring Himself out for the poor. He was all man and all
God, and yet He never used His power for selfish ambition, but instead He practised the true fast in
loosening, removing and breaking the yoke over people’s lives.
In the same way, God desires for us to serve the poor wholeheartedly and unselfishly, just as He
did and when we fulfil the true fast like this we are actually ministering to Him.
         Matthew 25:35,36 “For I was hungry, and you gave Me something to eat; I was thirsty, and
         you gave Me something to drink; I was a stranger, and you invited Me in; naked, and you
         clothed Me; I was sick, and you visited Me; I was in prison, and you came to Me.”
Isaiah 58 is a wake-up call for the body of Christ to get into action, to stop using the misconstrued
theology of “those are just works”, while continuing to walk in self-righteous comfort.
Read James 2:14-26. Works do not justify us, as we are justified by our faith, but if our faith is
without works, then we do not have true faith anyway. True faith, of full persuasion, must have an
outworking of works as commanded to do. Without acts of mercy and love, you are like “clanging
gongs” (1 Corinthians 13) and “cups cleaned on the outside but filthy on the inside” (Matthew
23:26). Faith without works is dead and not faith at all.
God reveals His true fast in verse 6 – to loosen, to undo and break every yoke.
What exactly is a yoke?
A yoke is a heavy wooden implement used to harness animals together, usually oxen. Thereafter,
other implements such as ploughs, sleds or trailers are attached to this yoke, which will be drawn
by the pulling power from the animals.




                                                                                               [12]
A yoke, or heavy implement, is burdensome to bear, heavy and uncomfortable to draw other
implements with, and worse still, is never liberating. The same holds true for poverty, it is heavy to
live with, difficult to produce anything and seemingly impossible to come out of.
The Isaiah 58 mandate to loosen, remove and break every yoke is a three-part process and cannot
be done in one motion.
1) You first loosen the bands or ropes, which tie the oxen to the yoke and the yoke to the
implement they are drawing. These bands are what will keep the oxen attached to the actual yoke
and implement being drawn. This allows the oxen to be given more breathing space and room to
move more freely, without implement attachments. Untying them doesn’t free them from the yoke
but brings freedom a little closer.
2) Then you undo and remove the yoke from the oxen’s neck by pulling out the U shaped wooden
inserts which the oxen pull against.
3) Then you break it, to ensure that it cannot be put on again.


                                               22
In the “Introduction”, great lengths were taken to explain the humanitarian crisis worldwide. God’s
true fast for us, His people who are called by His name, the House of Jacob, is to let the
oppressed go free.
Practically how do we, as God’s people, loosen, remove and break the yoke and let the oppressed
go free?
Divide your bread with the hungry
Shelter the homeless
Clothe the naked
Do not hide your eyes from the plight of others
Stop the pointing of the finger
Stop the speaking of wickedness
Give yourself to the hungry
Satisfy the desire of the afflicted
         Galatians 5:1 “It was for freedom that Christ set us free;”
The yoke is first and foremost a spiritual yoke and we must be fully persuaded of this if we want to
bring true deliverance to the captives. When Jesus said He came to give us life and life more
abundantly (John 10:10), He had this very same heart motive of fulfilling His Father’s true fast.
         Matthew 11:28-30 "Come to Me, all who are weary and heavy-laden, and I will give you rest.
         Take My yoke upon you and learn from Me, for I am gentle and humble in heart, and you will
         find rest for your souls. For My yoke is easy and My burden is light."
When we come to Him, we also receive a yoke, but unlike the yoke of the evil one, it is easy and
light. The yoke we take on is His yoke, not ours. He has already walked the walk under its weight
and taken the brunt of the curse on our behalf, so that we can walk in the freedom that comes from
His victory.
In order to take on Jesus’ yoke, we must first have the yoke of sin and death taken off our necks.
We do this spiritually by leading people through confession and repentance and thereafter, by faith,
the appointment of Jesus as Lord and Saviour. With this precious gift of salvation, we have the
right to be called children of God, and have our names written in the book of eternal life. What a
privilege!!!
Jesus is called the chief cornerstone and salvation is the placement of the cornerstone in the
temple of our lives. With this cornerstone in place, we can build our lives into a temple worthy of
His name, with every facet of our lives being built around Him. We have to start at the beginning,
with the beginning, Jesus - our strong deliverer and our hope. If we don’t build on Him, we labour in
vain. We cannot deny Him. Instead we must acknowledge Him in all of our ways, words and
demonstrations. That is why we call it Farming God's Way after all. It is an unashamed gospel of
the Kingdom of God in the domain of agriculture.
Consider that 1 in 10 people worldwide and 1 in 5 people in Africa are undernourished. Even if we
