   20.3.       With Minimal Wastage
Biblically, the most incredible demonstration of Jesus’ practise of minimal wastage, was shown at
the feeding of the five thousand.
         Matthew 14:14-21 “When He went ashore, He saw a large crowd, and felt compassion for
         them and healed their sick. When it was evening, the disciples came to Him and said, "This
         place is desolate and the hour is already late; so send the crowds away, that they may go
         into the villages and buy food for themselves." But Jesus said to them, "They do not need to
         go away; you give them something to eat!" They said to Him, "We have here only five loaves
         and two fish." And He said, "Bring them here to Me." Ordering the people to sit down on the
         grass, He took the five loaves and the two fish, and looking up toward heaven, He blessed
         the food, and breaking the loaves He gave them to the disciples, and the disciples gave
         them to the crowds, and they all ate and were satisfied. They picked up what was left over of
         the broken pieces, twelve full baskets. There were about five thousand men who ate,
         besides women and children.”
This miracle story has been told many times as a sign of Jesus’ miraculous power and authority
and yet I wonder how many times the collection of the wastage has even featured in the story. The
fact that they collected 12 baskets of leftover food clearly shows that man is wasteful, casting aside
the things that we don’t need right at that moment, even if they come through such an amazing
miraculous sign. If we look around us in the world today, we see this wasteful attribute of man
clearly on display. So many of God’s gifts to us are wasted, and it matters to God. People don’t
care about wastage but God does. We need to have our character shaped by Him so that we will
be transformed more and more into His likeness. We need to learn that it is important to practise
minimal wastage.
         John 5:19 Jesus said “I only do that which I see My Father in heaven doing”.
By collecting the wasted pieces of loaves and fishes that had been discarded and practising
minimal wastage, Jesus displayed one of the secrets of His Fathers’ character.
God practises minimal wastage, but man is wasteful.




                                                Water Cycle                              [16]




                                                   145
In creation there are so many demonstrations of this Godly character attribute of minimal wastage,
that it is impossible to ignore.
The energy for driving all of the cycles in creation, is provided by the sun. Short wave radiation
enters the atmosphere, heating the oceans and the earth’s surface, driving the ocean currents,
winds and forming the basis for all climates. The short wave is then given back into the
atmosphere as long wave radiation, which provides the heat for all life forms to have a suitable
environment to survive.
The heat causes evaporation from the oceans, lakes and rivers and evapo-transpiration from plant
leaves, all of which contribute to atmospheric moisture, which cools as it ascends higher to forms
clouds and eventually precipitation. In this water cycle there is no wastage of water. Rain that falls
has its kinetic energy broken by God’s Blanket and because of all the life in the soil, it permeates
deep into the soil profile, with only minimal runoff losses. Even deep water storage systems like
aquifers and ice fields of the polar ice caps are simply long term storage facilities for the water
cycle. Nothing gets wasted out of this system. This is God’s character attribute of minimal wastage
on display.




                                    Carbon Cycle                       [17]
Plants take up carbon dioxide and by using the energy of short wave sunlight they manufacture
carbohydrates, and release oxygen as a by-product. This process is called photosynthesis and is
the basis for all life on earth. Without this God-created production of carbohydrates from CO2, no
other life would have the building blocks to survive. Plants get eaten by animals and birds in the
food chain, which utilise the energy from the carbohydrates and give off carbon dioxide back into
the atmosphere as a by-product. Some of the carbon in animals gets transferred into the soil
through faeces or death and this will also eventually make its way back into the cycle. Plants that
die will get decomposed by bacteria and fungi and release carbon dioxide back into the cycle.
Even dead plant material that was buried during flooding or earth movements from volcanoes or
shifting continental plates, formed the fossil fuels, which we use to drive the technology of our
modern world.
God in His amazing wisdom has designed His creation in such a way that there are circles to all
forms of life, which are all interwoven with each other in a delicate balance in which nothing gets
wasted. God’s glory is clearly evident in His creation. If there is any meddling in this delicate
balance, the circles of life get distorted and require hundreds of years to allow the equilibrium to be
brought back into balance.



                                                   146
                                     Mozambiquean Deforestation

Deforestation
Large scale deforestation is a classic example of the destruction of this equilibrium through man’s
greed, where he takes without giving back. The forests are being destroyed by slash and burn
agriculture, charcoal making, firewood and timber exports. All over the world, the scars of what
were once beautiful forests, are now jungles of exotic shrubs and weeds. These sensitive
indigenous forests are our national heritage and should be preserved and protected for future
generations, not wasted for short term financial gain.
According to the FAO, we are losing 10 million hectares of indigenous forest per year across the
globe. In the last 100 years, more than half of the world’s forests or approximately 800 million
hectares have been deforested. The rate of deforestation is unsustainable as the global demand
for agricultural land, timber and wood fuels keeps on escalating.
Besides the obvious value in providing safe havens for our indigenous plants, birds, animals and
microbiology, the forests have a far more important long term role. Their preservation is extremely
important for the stability of our climate, both on a national and global level.
Deforestation has had a catastrophic effect on the environment and the situation is worsening daily
because of man’s wastage of this precious resource:
Firstly, the short wave rays of light from the sun hit the earth, instead of being intercepted by forest
leaves, and what was once productive photosynthetic light is now wasted, which bounces off the
ground as long wave radiation, which is responsible for the heating of the earth’s atmosphere.
Secondly, the carbon dioxide levels in the atmosphere increase, as a result of less photosynthesis
taking place and because of the burning up of organic matter. Carbon dioxide is a greenhouse gas
and absorbs significant amounts of long wave radiation, which in turn causes increased
atmospheric temperatures. According to research on the ice fields, carbon dioxide levels are
reported to have increased from 278 parts per million (ppm) in the pre-industrial era of the year
1780 to 417ppm in the year 2021, which is a 50% increase. The first 50 parts per million (ppm)
took nearly 200 years but the last 90 ppm increase has taken place in just 41 years. This increase
is mainly due to deforestation and the burning of fossil fuels and the significance of the role that
farmers have to play in deforestation cannot be ignored.
Thirdly, the indigenous forests play a significant role in harvesting rainfall in association with God’s
Blanket and then transpire that moisture back into the atmosphere very efficiently, increasing the
atmospheric moisture levels and therefore rainfall again. Deforestation has been proven to be a
major cause of drier climates, reduced rainfall, flash flooding and high soil erosion.


                                                 147
This deforestation example shows just how delicately God’s creation is interwoven and how the
greed of man and his wastage can destroy God’s order. These sensitive forest ecosystems will
take hundreds of years to recover, if they are ever given the chance. The remaining 4 billion
hectares of forest, which are all photosynthesizing every day, have a significant role to play in this
delicate balance and emphasise the need for us to actively preserve them.
Many amazing organizations worldwide are lobbying against deforestation and motivating tree
planting. We need to get this message out as the body of Christ, so that He will find us to be good
stewards of the beautiful creation He has entrusted us with.
You might say: What does this have to with farming? Slash and burn agriculture is currently being
practised in many countries across the continent. It involves the deforestation of natural indigenous
forests, either through burning or logging, with new land areas being opened up for agriculture
every day. The lands get abused and within no time at all those same lands are regarded as
wastelands and more deforestation occurs. Although hard to believe, many countries suffering
from food insecurity were originally completely forested, and now they cannot feed themselves.
Once beautiful productive forests are now wastelands where people are desperately trying to eke
out a living.
However, preserving the indigenous forests at the expense of people’s lives is not a solution. We
must find ways for farmers to live in harmony with God’s creation.
1) By practicing Farming God's Way, farmers can feed their families and make profits out of
smaller tracts of land, hereby reducing the overall demand on land because it is done to high
standards. Example: Pius Mutie in Mpeketoni, Kenya, used to grow maize on 6 acres and
harvested only 20 bags. After turning to Farming God's Way he now only grows maize on 2 acres
and gets 40 bags. This is only the beginning for Pius, but it illustrates the minimal wastage principle
exceptionally well. Farming large tracts of land at a low return is wastage.
2) Farmers are also able to make sustainable profits from the same tract of land, through good
“Sowing and Reaping” Biblical principles. This way we can preserve the indigenous forests without
causing the poor to suffer, simply for conservation’s sake. Currently the demand for agricultural
land is so high that marginal lands unsuitable for cultivation, like steep slopes and erodable soils,
are being farmed. By improving yields on smaller tracts of land, we can effectively eliminate the
cultivation of marginal lands, which can instead be kept for forestry and pasture use instead.
3) Agroforestry - Farmers can also be taught the skills of growing forestry crops on dedicated
portions of their fields. This is called agro-forestry and can provide sufficient charcoal or firewood
for cooking, as well as the timber needed for home construction. Replacing the thinned trees would
allow for a sustainable timber supply for all of the farming family’s needs. There are proactive ways
for farmers to not waste the precious resource of the indigenous forests.

Minimising Soil, Moisture and Nutrient Wastage
Another area where agriculture is guilty of significant wastage is soil erosion. Leading soil scientists
in Africa estimate soil losses at between 55 and 250 tons of soil per hectare per year. That is
between 1,100 bags and 5,000 bags of soil lost as compared to the average maize yield of 7 bags
per hectare. This is wastage and unfaithfulness. The wastage all comes from man leaning on his
own understanding, ploughing and burning crop residues.
The trials done at the agricultural research station in Cedara, South Africa clearly demonstrated
that with just one storm trial, conventionally farmed land lost 90% of the rainfall through runoff,
taking along with it 28.5 tons of top soil per hectare through sheet erosion. Whereas the land that
had no ploughing and a mere 80% cover of God’s Blanket, only lost 6% of the rainfall and 1 ton of
soil per hectare. Many people joke about soil being Africa’s greatest export but this is testimony to
our unfaithfulness with the land that He has entrusted to us. Soil erosion is an indicator that we do
not appreciate the gift that God has given and shows our high levels of wastage. The wastage of
soils results in them getting shallower and less fertile, until finally the topsoil is gone and only the
poor lower horizons of soil remain. With this kind of wastage, it is impossible to make sustainable
profits. The wastage of rainfall and soil has consequences for the whole ecosystem downstream,
from flash flooding to siltation of watercourses, amongst others.



                                                  148
God’s Blanket is the primary key that God has given us to minimise the wastage of soil, moisture
and nutrients besides its many other benefits. God’s Blanket is a farmer’s gold and I cannot think of
one legitimate reason why anyone would burn this precious resource.


Minimising Sunlight Wastage
Every one of the steps within the Farming God's Way sequence have been carefully chosen to
ensure the principles of minimal wastage are attained. It is such a simple sequence to follow step
by step, but the deeper reasoning behind this simplicity is very purposeful and complex.
For example the spacing of 60 by 75cm and a population density of 44,444 makes sure that the
crop “Closes the Canopy” early, which is important to allow the crop to capture as much of the
available sunlight for photosynthesis as possible, whilst utilising limited land area to best potential.


Minimising Inputs Wastage
The traditional practise of broadcasting nutrients like fertiliser and manure, dilutes and wastes our
inputs, giving the weeds a better chance of success than our crop. In Farming God's Way, we are
minimising the wastage of inputs like woodash, anthill soil, manure, compost or fertiliser by placing
them directly in each hole or furrow, allowing the roots of the crop to gain quick access to the
available nutrients.


Minimising Time Wastage
Although time is a management key, it is also a resource to consider. Ploughing is not only
destructive, but also a significant waste of precious time. I have enjoyed many testimonies of
Farming God's Way farmers spending half the time on land preparation, as they don’t have to
plough anymore and spending triple the time on harvesting and shelling because of the radical
yield improvements.
In our detailed comparative time trials, we have collected data on each field operation with Farming
God's Way “land preparation costs at 30%” and “weeding costs at 28%” as compared to
Conventional Farming. This dramatic saving of time, from these two factors alone, will save time
and money and give farmers a real chance to accomplish sustainable profitability.
Commercially these numbers also hold true, with Conventional commercial farmers spending 3
times the amount of money on land preparation than those practising no ploughing. When you
consider the high prices of diesel, oil and maintenance of expensive machinery, it seems like folly
to continue practising such high financial wastage.
God can redeem anything from waste to promise, even dongas that are 20m deep or expansive
deforested areas or degraded soils. In an area called Tomali in Malawi, a Well Watered Garden
was planted into a lifeless soil which was as hard as concrete. After 1 year, when replanting the
garden, one could put your fingers into that same soil and after 2 years, the soil colour and texture
had changed miraculously. The soil was dark, crumbly and full of organic matter, with many
airspaces and channels from the insects, which had been busily transforming a waste land into a
land with signs of promise.
         Isaiah 51:3 “For the Lord shall comfort Zion: he will comfort all her waste places; and he will
         make her wilderness like Eden, and her desert like the garden of the Lord; joy and gladness
         shall be found therein, thanksgiving, and the voice of melody.”
Just outside the garden where man’s way was continuing, the soil remained in a state of waste -
degraded, lifeless and with no hope. The Lord is an exacting master and His word says that if we
are unfaithful even with little, that He will take it away from us. God will judge each of us according
to the measure He has given us and if we are unfaithful with it, He will give it to someone more
faithful than ourselves.
Be encouraged to assess all areas of your life and farming practise, and to align them with the
Godly attribute of minimal wastage.



                                                     149
    20.4.       With the Fruit of the Spirit
Farming God's Way is about equipping the poor to be able to come out from under the yoke of
oppression and to be able to walk in the fullness of Jesus’ promised abundant life. Farming God's
Way is made up of 3 spheres of influence, Biblical, Management and Technology, which are all
interwoven into 1 thread. All 3 of these spheres all work together, to help to transform lives and
livelihoods in the agricultural domain including the essential 4th management key – with the Fruit of
the Holy Spirit.
Our management objective in Farming God's Way is Sustainable Profitability, where we are
trusting God for improved profits, through increasing our yields; reducing our costs; and spreading
our risk. In this chapter, we will reveal the vital influence that “the fruit of the Spirit” has in ensuring
sustainable profitability in our lives.
Harvest time is the culmination of all of the hard work and effort that we put into farming coming to
fruition, and is supposed to be a time of rejoicing with family and friends, as we celebrate the fruit
of our hard labour. It should also be a time of honouring God with our tithes and offerings; repaying
our debts; investing our increase; and being a blessing to those less fortunate than ourselves.
However, harvest time is often not like that in reality.
The following harvest time stories illustrate just how strong the association between the Fruit of the
Spirit and sustainable profitability really is, and that without the Fruit of the Spirit, there can be no
lasting blessing.
When I was on a mission in Kenya in 2006, I picked up a local Kenyan newspaper and read with
shock of the mass migration of prostitutes up to Narok, in advance of the expected bumper wheat
harvest in the region. The newspaper explained that many farmers would even have taken bank
loans and spent it in bars and with prostitutes, even before harvesting a grain of wheat! I felt
heartbroken for the farmers and that their hard work would come to nothing in the end. The smell
of the upcoming wheat revenue had been sniffed out by sex workers who used their cunning and
wiles, to seduce the farmers to part with their hard earned rewards. What a travesty!
However, as I researched it further, I realised that this was not a once off event at all, but instead
an annual event, and is as much a social/cultural migration as the annual Wildebeest migration in
the Masai Mara. The Narok area accounts for nearly half of the 500,000 tonnes of wheat produced
locally and the commercial sex workers come like leaches during wheat harvesting seasons to
drain farm bank accounts. The prostitutes come from all over Kenya – Nakuru, Eldoret, Kericho,
Nairobi, Central Kenya and Mombasa and even as far as Uganda and Tanzania.
As if that wasn’t enough of an agricultural catastrophe, the research also reveals that the
prostitutes move to Kericho during the tea bonus season, and then on to Eldoret during the maize
harvest; then to Thika for the pineapple harvest and finally close the agricultural migration loop in
Naivasha with the cut flower farmers.
The harsh reality of these annual regional migrations should not invoke our pity for farmers; after
all, if there was no demand, then the prostitutes would not be there in the first place. This demand
is clearly established by the insatiable desire for illegitimate sexual pleasure by the farmers
themselves, and has become so consistent and well established over many, many years, that it
has formed a social migratory pattern amongst the prostitution rings. Can you imagine the torment
of the farmers’ wives and families, who know what is to come around harvest time each and every
year…the strife in the homes, high incidence of divorce, wrecked families and bankruptcy. These
are the fruits of harvest time.
Another harvest time story is of how an agricultural development company in Uganda, developed
an outgrower strategy with vanilla, to empower local farming communities. The agri-development
company had good intentions to help the poor; however, the outcomes received were shocking to
say the least… In their first season, there was an international spike in the vanilla price, which went
nearly 10 times higher. Their farmers made a fortune, but it was these very same riches, that
caused havoc in these communities. Many farmers left their families and homes, relocated to
cities, lived in brothels, got involved with prostitutes and even married them. They exhausted their
cash on non-essential items, fleeting pleasures and expensive vehicles and when it started running


                                                   150
out, they returned back home, and in some cases even kicked out their wives and children. This
was a community disaster, not a development.
Riches without values is not wealth, it’s like fruit that has gone rotten. Without Christian character
we will never have any longevity from our fruitfulness or profitability. We need to have the “Fruit of
the Spirit” in our lives.
         Psalm 37:35 “I have seen a wicked, violent man spreading himself like a luxuriant tree in its
         native soil. Then he passed away, and lo, he was no more; I sought for him, but he could not
         be found.”
There may be rich people in your community, whose lives look so appealing, and they spread
themselves out like a luxuriant tree, but watch and see, behold one day, they will be no more. This
is because Christian character and moral fibre are not a part of their nature, and their riches will
erode and come to nothing. We need to have the fruit of the Spirit in our lives.
         Mark 8:36 “For what does it profit a man to gain the whole world, and forfeit his soul?”
When we set targets of fruitfulness, improved yields and livelihoods, it is essential to consider the
“whole person” in this journey. This is why the Biblical keys play such an important role in the roll
out of Farming God's Way.
         Psalm 37:16-20 “Better is the little of the righteous than the abundance of many wicked. For
         the arms of the wicked will be broken, but the Lord sustains the righteous. The Lord knows
         the days of the blameless, and their inheritance will be forever. They will not be ashamed in
         the time of evil, and in the days of famine they will have abundance. But the wicked will
         perish.”
It is very clear from scripture that many who are first in this world, will be last in the Kingdom of
God and many who are last in this world, will be first in the Kingdom of God. We are exhorted to be
righteous and blameless, storing up for ourselves treasures in heaven, where moth and rust do not
destroy, nor where thieves break in and steal, because these treasures are eternal, and leave an
inheritance that lasts forever. Sustainable profitability is dependent on our God’s commanded
blessing, which comes as a result of the fruit of our lives.
Dickson Shuwali’s Farming God's Way success story is an incredible one indeed, but most people
don’t know the start of his journey. Dickson grew up very poor, often eating a meal every second
day! The very reason for the abject poverty comes because of the fact that his family were not
walking in God’s ways. His father was a polygamist, well known as a drunkard, and for his
promiscuous behavior in the area, as well as being heavily involved in ancestral worship and
witchcraft. According to scripture, we are either in the Kingdom of light or the kingdom of darkness,
and this family were living and eating of the fruit of the kingdom of darkness with poverty, hunger
and strife. The entry for Dickson to come into the Kingdom of light was when he came to know the
Lord Jesus Christ as Lord and Saviour, and thereafter as he began to implement Farming God's
Way. He went from his father’s average 3 bags acre to 5 bags to 45 bags to 54 bags and 69 bags
in a four year period, on his father’s same tract of ground. It’s a wonderful story of God’s
redemptive plan and promised abundance.
         John 3:1-6 “Now there was a man of the Pharisees, named Nicodemus, a ruler of the Jews;
         this man came to Jesus by night and said to Him, "Rabbi, we know that You have come from
         God as a teacher; for no one can do these signs that You do unless God is with him." Jesus
         answered and said to him, "Truly, truly, I say to you, unless one is born again he cannot see
         the Kingdom of God." Nicodemus said to Him, "How can a man be born when he is old? He
         cannot enter a second time into his mother's womb and be born, can he?" Jesus answered,
         "Truly, truly, I say to you, unless one is born of water and the Spirit he cannot enter into the
         Kingdom of God. That which is born of the flesh is flesh, and that which is born of the Spirit is
         spirit.””
We were all born of the flesh and we were born in sin, but to be born again we need to be born of
the Spirit. The first Adam failed by succumbing to temptation in the Garden of Eden, but Jesus, the
second Adam, was triumphant over sin and death. To be born of the Spirit is the most amazing gift
of God to mankind, because He sacrificed His own Son, Jesus Christ, to restore us into
rightstanding with Himself.


                                                     151
         John 3:16 “For God so loved the world, that He gave His only begotten Son, that whoever
         believes in Him shall not perish, but have eternal life.”
Have you submitted your life to the Lordship of Jesus Christ? If you haven’t and your life is filled
with bad and immoral fruit that you are not proud of, then today is the day of your salvation. You
need to confess your sins, repent from them, and pray and ask God to be the Lord of your life.
         1 John 1:9 “If we confess our sins, He is faithful and just to forgive us our sins and to cleanse
         us from all unrighteousness.”
Twenty years ago, I collected a giant seed from a beautiful large tree at a mission station that I
visited many times in Mozambique. The seed symbolizes so much potential, but it needs to be
planted to become something of its genetic potential. Jesus is called the ‘incorruptible seed’, and
when you accept Him as your Lord and Saviour He becomes planted into your life. This is the point
of Salvation when you establish the Lordship of Jesus Christ in your life.
         1 Peter 1:23 “For you have been born again not of seed which is corruptible, but
         incorruptible, that is, through the living and enduring word of God.”
The incorruptible seed, Christ in you, cannot be corrupted, as He is the spotless Lamb of God, and
no matter what you do, He will remain pure and righteous and we too are made pure and righteous
because of His finished work on the cross.
         2 Corinthians 5:21 “He made Him who knew no sin to be sin on our behalf, that we might
         become the righteousness of God in Him.”
However, Jesus doesn’t want His seed in you to just remain a seed. He wants that seed to become
a deep rooted and well established tree, which in turn will yield beautiful fruit, as evidence of His
transformation of your own life. You need to study the Word of God, pray regularly and find mature
believers in your church or community to walk with you and disciple you, so that the incorruptible
seed in you can grow and so that you can become well established in your faith.
         Galatians 5:16,17 “But I say, walk by the Spirit, and you will not carry out the desire of the
         flesh. For the flesh sets its desire against the Spirit, and the Spirit against the flesh; for these
         are in opposition to one another, so that you may not do the things that you please.”
Picture two bowls of fruit, which represent the opposition between the fruit of the flesh on the one
hand and the fruit of the Spirit on the other hand.
The 1st bowl is filled with disgusting, soggy, mouldy fruit and is really not something you want to
even touch, never mind eat, and you certainly would not put this out for your family.
The 2nd bowl by comparison, is filled with beautiful, tasty, shiny, colourful fruit and is really
appealing. You know even before eating of it, that it is good for you and your family.
Now, the challenge for you today is, “is the fruit of the flesh or the fruit of the Holy Spirit in evidence
in your life?” because the flesh and the Spirit are in opposition to each other all the time.
         Matthew 7:16-18 “By their fruits ye shall know them. Do men gather grapes of thorns, or figs
         of thistles? So every good tree bears good fruit, but the bad tree bears bad fruit. A good tree
         cannot produce bad fruit, nor can a bad tree produce good fruit.”
What kind of fruit is your life bearing?
What kind of fruit is evidenced in your family?
What kind of fruit is evidenced in your community?
What kind of fruit is evidenced in your nation?
My encouragement to you today is to examine the fruit in your life. Acknowledge what is not of God
and let the Holy Spirit convict you to sort that out, so that you can align your ways with God’s ways.
         Galatians 5:19-21a “Now the deeds of the flesh are evident, which are: immorality, impurity,
         sensuality, idolatry, sorcery, enmities, strife, jealousy, outbursts of anger, disputes,

Is the Holy Spirit highlighting any bad fruits like these in your life? If so, don’t be ashamed, just be
honest in identifying them and diligent in rooting them out of your life.

         Matthew 7:19-20 “Every tree that does not bear good fruit is cut down and thrown into the

As we look around our communities, we see so much bad fruit, and that in turn bears even more

darkness. We have got to change our ways.
         Galatians 5:21b “I forewarn you, just as I have forewarned you, that those who practice such

Anyone who is in Christ has a love and compassion for the poor, where we long to see the cycle of

align our ways to God’s ways. God calls us and says:
         Zechariah 1:3 “Return to Me, that I may return to you, says the Lord of hosts.”
         Matthew 23:12 “Whoever exalts himself shall be humbled, and whoever humbles himself
         shall be exalted.”
Where do you stand today? What fruit is your life bearing? Are you prepared to humble yourself
before the King of kings and the Lord of lords?
An amazing part of the story with Dickson’s father is that, although the fruit of his life was all of the
bad fruits of the flesh, he humbled himself and surrendered his life to the Lordship of Jesus Christ.
He became a man of faith. He went through discipleship training and was filled with the Holy Spirit.
He has one wife and is exercising Godly leadership in his home. Where there was shame and
dishonour, there is now respect and honour. Where his family was ripped apart and fragmented
now the Lord has brought healing and unity. In the community where there was disrepute, poverty,
hunger and begging, the Lord has restored him as a statesman and a respected elder in the
community. This is God’s heart to redeem all things and he can do the same in your life.
It may seem like an impossible journey to go from bearing bad fruit to good fruit, but this is the very
nature of the Father heart of God. He has such a deep love for those that are broken, lost and
separated from Him. He has done everything He can, so that we can come out of the disgusting,
immoral kingdom of satan, even sending His only begotten, pure and spotless Son, Jesus, to die
on a cross to take away the sin of the world. The journey is not an impossible journey. Everybody
can make this transition, if we are prepared to get grafted in to His family and fruitfulness.
         John 15:1-8 “I am the true vine, and My Father is the Vinedresser. Every branch in Me that

The evidence of the state of your life is made very clear by the kind of fruit that you bear. If you

Would anyone come and eat from a bowl of disgusting, rotten bad fruit…obviously not.

How do we apply the fruit of the Spirit in our everyday agricultural practices?

When we bear good fruit there will be a consequence of that good fruit into our own personal lives

Hunger will be gone, because the Bible says that “His children will never beg bread.”
People will live healthy lives with no sexually transmitted diseases, because His children will be
behaving in a sexually moral way. Nations would be transformed, because there would be no
corruption, stealing, crime, rape or murder.
After training in Johannesburg, in South Africa, many years ago I had a young Zimbabwean girl
come to me at the break after the practical. With much excitement she said “I have seen this
Farming God's Way done before in my home village”. As you can imagine I was just as excited and
said “wow that’s so great”. She said “No, no Mr. Grant, it's not great at all. There was a man in our
village who did this digging of planting stations and used the blanket like you just showed us and
he was getting fantastic yields. But when we used to go anywhere near his field he would swear at
us and throw stones at us. He would even threaten to send the witchdoctors to curse us and our
families if we ever went near his field ever again.”
Now just imagine this situation, where someone with a little knowledge of the technology keys,
would not want to share what he knows and instead threaten people that even tried it. What kind of
a testimony is that for Farming God's Way, and what fruit is in evidence in this man’s life?



                                                      154
In Farming God's Way, we want to see God’s Master plan for mankind to be revealed, where we
will be in heartfelt relationship with God, with one another, and with the land, where His fullness

only bearing fruit with the technology, but the fruit of your life is bad, then what you are doing is
NOT Farming God's Way.
The heart behind Farming God's Way is that we would be able to extend love to the least of these,

hoarding up riches for ourselves, not hiding God’s providence and hope from those that need it the
most in our communities.
This is such an exciting opportunity for us to bring transformation from the bottom up. It starts with
each one of us bearing the fruit of the Holy Spirit out of a heartfelt relationship with the Lord.
         Galatians 5:25 “If we live by the Spirit, let us also walk by the Spirit.”
If you want to have God’s sustainable profitability to be commanded as a blessing over your farm
fields, then you must walk according to the Spirit, live by the Spirit and bear fruit in accordance with
the Spirit. God is an exacting master and He will come and He will examine the fruit on the tree of
your life. If he finds fruit not in accordance with the Kingdom of God, you will be cut down and your
riches will come to nought. But if God comes and He finds beautiful fruit in accordance with the
Fruit of the Holy Spirit, just like the fruit Jesus demonstrated while He was on earth, He will
command a blessing. That fruit in turn will bear fruit and seed after its own kind and cause a great
multiplication and we will see lives, families, farms, communities and nations transformed from the
bottom up. Live by the Spirit, walk according to the Spirit and bear the fruit of the Holy Spirit.

Management Summary
Remember, everything in life is not only about sustainability or making a profit, as many are the
rich who die in their misery, so keep the balance between increase and godliness. Our aspirations
for good management and therefore “Sustainable Profitability” are secondary to a walk worthy of
being called His children. We need to do everything we do as unto the Lord, with all the fruits of the
Holy Spirit evident as a witness of God’s transforming power in our lives.
Every place where farmers have wholeheartedly implemented Farming God's Way, they have
achieved a radical God-given improvement in their yields. As they continue to grow and improve
their management standards yearly, they go on a journey of their land being redeemed, soils being
healed, abundance revealed and lives transformed into more and more of His likeness.
In summary of management then, we are aiming to achieve sustainable profitability by doing things
on time, to high standards, with minimal wastage and with the fruit of the Holy Spirit.

21. Comparative Garden Data

Comparative Gardens are probably the most effective visual aid that we have to be able to
encourage trainees, as they dramatically showcase the benefits of Farming God's Way as
compared to Conventional practices. There are currently Farming God's Way comparative gardens
in many countries and in many formats, from simple pairs to multiple complex agronomic and
vegetable comparative matrices. Trainers are heartily encouraged to set these up and manage
them to high standards, because they are such a great visual aid with excellent extension value.
The Port Elizabeth comparative garden matrix is a comparison of Farming God's Way plots versus
Conventional plots; with equal water and inputs; using maize and green beans; with manure and
compost. Each of the plots are managed to exacting standards and meticulous timings are taken
for every operation. The costs and timings taken from the sample plots are later extrapolated to
costs per hectare.

Conventional Plot Preparation
The conventional plots are ploughed by hand to 20cm deep, using hoes. We start at the bottom
and cut a 20cm deep furrow into the last row and then work our way up furrow by furrow, until the
whole plot is completed. We make sure to invert the soil by throwing the soil in the new furrow into
the completed furrow. The hand ploughing of a hectare would take a farmer at least 21 days of
very hard labour, which would probably not be sustainable, so will more likely end up taking 4-6
weeks.
In the conventional maize plots, we count out 99 x 500ml bowls of manure or compost and then
evenly broadcast these inputs on top of the ground, as would traditionally be done.
We apply 54 x 500ml bowls in the Conventional Bean plots.
The inputs are then lightly worked into the ground a few centimetres deep, and then the soil is
raked level.
Planting stations for maize are dug at 60 by 75cm intervals and 5cm deep for the Conventional
Farming plots. Furrows for Conventional Farming beans are dug 3cm deep across each 75cm row.
This last phase of the actual planting stations and furrow preparation is very fast in the
conventional plots, because of the previously ploughed soil and we only need to prepare to the
seeding depth as the inputs have already been mixed in.




                                                                                                                                            156
Farming God's Way Plot Preparation
The Farming God's Way plots have not been ploughed for many, many seasons now. Not
ploughing a Farming God's Way hectare would cost a farmer absolutely nothing.
In the Farming God's Way maize plots, we move the blanket about 30cm down slope from the
measuring rope mark, then dig the planting stations 15cm deep and at 60 by 75cm intervals. A
tablespoon of woodash is placed in each planting station. It is recommended that you place
350-500ml of manure or compost per planting station, depending on whether you will be applying a
top dressing of Urea or not. We know that most poor small scale farmers do not have access to
fertiliser and therefore we place a 500ml bowl of manure or compost in each planting station and
we do not top dress these plots at all. We then cover all the inputs with a 3cm soil separation layer
leaving 5cms, or the length of a matchbox, depth for planting.
When preparing the Farming God's Way plots for beans, the blanket is moved 30cm downslope
and furrows are dug 10cm deep, across each 75cm row line. A tablespoon of woodash is placed
every 60cm across the furrow. We recommend using 500ml of manure or compost per meter,
depending on the quality of your soils. All the inputs get covered with a 3cm soil separation layer,
leaving 3cms, or the width of a matchbox, depth for planting.

Planting
We presoak our maize seeds for 6 hours before planting and place 3 seeds in each planting station
at 5cm deep. One on the left, one in the middle and one on the right. Cover level.
Bean seeds are not pre-soaked. Seeds are placed 10cm apart in the 75cm rows and covered level.


Weeding
The Conventional Farming plots are always full of weeds because ploughed and naked soils are
the perfect environment for weeds to flourish in. Ploughing exposes weed seeds to sunlight and
the scarification of their seed coat stimulates weeds to germinate. Naked soil is also the perfect
environment for problematic creeping grasses like kweek / Cynodon and kikuyu to fulfil their God
designed function as soil stabilisers, and therefore there is a constant battle against them. The
Conventional plots get weeded at least 3 times in the growing season, and another 3 times in the
off season, just to prevent having a jungle of weeds over winter time, but we only take account for
the summer weedings in the budget, otherwise it would just make the budget cost simply
ridiculous. Weeding under Conventional Farming is done to 5cm deep to replicate what is
traditionally done.
God’s Blanket has the potential to reveal God’s promised abundance in our fields, but most
farmers, without this knowledge, reject this amazing treasure by burning or burying it. To those that
appreciate it, God’s Blanket becomes a real blessing and provides the ideal environment for the
healing of the land to take place.
The Farming God's Way plots have not been weeded for 5 years. If there is insufficient blanket
cover, then add supplementary blanket when the plants are one foot tall. We count how long it
takes to apply the blanket and include the cost of the blanket in our budgeting. The plants soon
close canopy, and although the blanket will start to decompose over summer, the closed canopy
will suppress light penetration and weed growth. As the maize starts to dry off, cover crops are
oversown under the maize canopy. There are many types of cover crops around these days, but
we prefer small grains that produce a lot of biomass, with a legume for Nitrogen fixation. Our
favourite is a 30:70 ratio of Vetch and Triticale but sometimes substitute Triticale with Stooling Rye
grass or Black Oats. We leave these cover crops to grow through the entire winter and kill them at
the point when their seeds are at the milky dough stage so that they die and do not reseed. Cover
crops are brilliant at controlling weeds, producing new blanket material for the coming season,
fixing nitrogen, recycling nutrients, preventing the soil from ever being naked and exposed to the
elements and creating an environment which encourages biodiversity of soil organisms. Many
people ask where they are going to get enough blanket material to cover their large fields.




                                                 157
God’s blanket will come from last season’s leftover crops; adding supplementary blanket; but most
effectively it should be grown in situ through cover cropping techniques. Cover cropping will
become one of the most influential shifts in agriculture in the next 20 years, as the drive to move
away from tillage and herbicides becomes more and more important.

Labour Costs
   For land preparation and planting, the Conventional Farming maize plots take 42 labour
    days/ha in comparison to Farming God's Way at just 24 days/ha. That is almost half the land
    preparation cost saved and will affect our sustainable profitability significantly!
   The Labour days comparisons for weeding are incredible, with Conventional Farming requiring
    a total of 34 days of weeding whereas Farming God's Way a total of only 9 days to add
    supplementary blanket cover and sow cover crops.
No matter what currency you are using, this is a total of 76 labour days with Conventional Farming
versus just 33 labour days with Farming God's Way, and a 43% saving in the cost of production.


Yield Differences
   The Farming God's Way Maize with Manure plots consistently yield 7-8t/ha as compared to the
    Conventional Farming Maize plots 3-4t/ha.
   The Farming God's Way Maize with Compost plots consistently yield 8-9t/ha as compared to
    the Conventional Farming Maize plots 3-4t/ha.
   The Conventional Farming green bean plots with compost yielded 3.2kg versus an incredible
    64.7kg from the Farming God's Way bean plot.
The reality is that these yield differences will get wider over the years as the Farming God's Way
plots improve and as the Conventional plots get worse and worse.
As you can see, the yield improvements under these controlled environments with equal water and
inputs are radically different and one would have to be foolish not to take note of the huge
improvements in yield, never mind the land stewardship benefits we have already covered.




                                                158
Profitability
Farming is a business and putting all these numbers into a spreadsheet, shows just how the cost
reductions and yield improvements affect the farm’s sustainable profitability. Although these figures
use the labour cost based on the South African minimum wage, which is much higher than in other
African countries, the principles apply irrespective of your currency.
   The Maize trials with Farming God's Way manure and compost plots were both able to make a
    profit. Whereas the Maize trials with Conventional plots were unable to make a profit in both
    cases. The Conventional Maize Manure plot making a loss of $-293/ha and the Conventional
    Compost plot losing $-402/ha.
   The Manure trials showed that Farming God's Way made $848/ha more profit than
    Conventional farming.
   The Compost trials showed that Farming God's Way made $1,331/ha more profit than
    Conventional farming.
   The Farming God's Way Compost trials consistently outcompeted the Farming God's Way
    Manure trials, making a strong case for the shift to a compost based system based on
    profitability, besides the many other benefits of compost as a basal input.


In Summary:
The Farming God's Way plots typically take half the time for land preparation and weeding as
compared to the Conventional plots.
The Farming God's Way maize plots consistently show 2.5 to 3 times yield improvements as
compared to the Conventional maize plots, whether using manure or compost.
The Farming God's Way green bean plots showed yield improvements of more than 20 times as
compared to the Conventional bean plots from the same area and using the same volume of
inputs.

If you ever wondered why there is such a flood of people leaving the rural areas and moving to find
work in the cities and towns around the world, here is your answer. Farmers are losing money
every season under Conventional Farming practises and because they cannot feed their families,
they leave to go and look for work in other countries and cities, even if they know they will only get
paid the minimum wage. The failed state of the agricultural sector has caused this urbanisation to
take place, but it is not because of farming but instead it is because of our ways.


         Ezekiel 18:25 "Yet you say, 'The way of the Lord is not right.' Hear now, O house of Israel! Is
         My way not right? Is it not your ways that are not right?”

22. Basic Budget

decisions on profitability. The table below is a sample budget based on maize figures from Malawi
with a dry bean rotation.

Although this is very hypothetical, it is really important to understand how these figures can be
affected by management decisions.




                                                     160
Example 1 - A case for compost:
It is assumed that the farmer has to buy manure in the basic budget example. If this same farmer
used 500ml of Farming God's Way compost and didn’t topdress with urea, he would save $167.60
in input costs and his yield potential would increase to 9t/ha, with profits increasing by $257.68
overall which is very significant. This encourages farmers to prepare the compost in the first
production year, to be ready for use in season 2. Most farm communities have large stock piles of
manure currently, but after 1 or 2 seasons of use, that reserve will be depleted. It is important to
start using the nitrogen source from the manure more wisely in good quality compost from early on
in the adoption of Farming God's Way.


Example 2 - High cost of contract labour:
If land owners are idle and use contract labour instead of working the fields themselves, then their
profitability will obviously decline. This is a great benefit of Farming God's Way, as land
preparations can be done by the family in stages before the rains come, so even the sick and
elderly can farm profitably.
         “One who works his land will have plenty of bread, but one who pursues worthless things
         lacks sense.” Proverbs 12:11
In the chapter “Comparative garden data” it is clearly shown that land preparation and weeding
labour days are cut in half by doing Farming God's Way as compared to Conventional farming,
with labour days further reduced by ensuring 100% cover with God’s Blanket 2.5cm thick. These
high standards ultimately all affect the bottom line.

Example 3 - igh yields don’t mean high profits:
If a farmer decides to chase increased yields by using high doses of inorganic fertilisers, his
income will increase to a point, but if the cost does not justify the yield improvement, then he could
even make a loss. This is the law of diminishing returns and there is no better example than with
agricultural inputs. Higher yields don’t always mean higher profits.
Yield is less important to the business of farming than profitability. Farming is a business with
profits coming from the difference between income and expenditure.
Inorganic fertiliser prices have skyrocketed in the past few years with phosphatic fertilisers
increasing over three times and Nitrates more than doubling, making the use of inorganics less and
less profitable. In areas with a high risk of crop failure from drought or flooding, the high inorganic
fertiliser costs will not be worth the financial risk. Carefully assess these cost and risk issues in
your budget planning. We strongly recommend high quality compost, which will both outcompete
inorganics in dry seasons and transform your soils in the long term, allowing you to become much
more sustainably profitable.
Understanding these figures and how they are affected by management decisions will assist
farmers to become more and more profitable. However, accurate record keeping of costs, crop
prices and market trends are essential to allow each farmer to sharpen his/her own budget and
operations.
Besides a production budget, it is also really important to have a personal budget that will allow
you to manage your personal finances in a Godly way. This will allow your family to keep out of
debt, manage your income and expenditure as well as allow you to save and accumulate wealth
over time.


                         You cannot manage what you do not measure!!!




                                                  161
23. Extension – Getting the Message Out

As Farming God's Way gets extended widely through the nations, our encouragement to you is
this… Never forget that the POOR are the target audience. They have a special place in God’s
heart and we must ensure that we keep focussed on serving them wholeheartedly.

1) It Starts with You
If you are going to become a Farming God's Way farmer, then go through the teachings thoroughly
and ensure that you start small and expand with faithfulness. Keep on studying the resources and
go to frequent training sessions.
Although anyone is able to facilitate and train Farming God’s Way due to it being a free resource
for the wider body of Christ to use, if you are serious about becoming a Farming God's Way
Accredited Trainer, then you need to commit yourself to going through the equipping and relational
accreditation process with a senior Accredited Trainer.
This includes both physical and oral credits including:
Attending at least 3 training events; Planting and managing your own Well Watered Garden for a
season; Participating in the in-field mentoring mission trips; Gaining 18 oral credits; a written
assignment. When one of the senior Farming God's Way trainers feels that you are ready, then we
will relationally accredit you as a Farming God's Way trainer.
Remember that Farming God's Way is not an organisation, but a tool given to the body of Christ.
We are not trying to build you into Farming God's Way, but rather to equip you to use Farming
God's Way. We want to release thousands of trainers to be able to extend this message of hope
under their own covering of church, mission or NGO. Contact your regional Accredited Trainer for
more information on accreditation.

2) Commitment of 5/6 Year Repeats
When breaking into a community really enquire of the Lord and ask Him where you should deliver
this message of hope. It requires great commitment and determination to go through the 3 stage
process of loosening, removing and breaking the yoke. In the team of trainers that I oversee, we
have a policy to practise 5 or 6 years of repeat training in a poor community. This commitment
comes at a high price, but we do it in obedience to what the Lord has spoken for us to do and this
has borne much fruit over the years. It is not good to do a once off training, as this can often do
more damage than good. Poverty cannot be broken through one training session.
In my experience, in the first year of training only the real agents of change in the community will
be prepared to fully implement Farming God's Way. Encourage hesitant farmers to at least try
Farming God’s Way in a portion of their field. I wish they would change immediately as they lose
out on a year’s advantages, but this is a process in communities, so like I have learnt – be patient.
In the second and third years, spend lots of time encouraging the farmers that tried it, giving them
the individual attention they need to get them to follow through with their persuasions. These
farmers will bring the testimonies that you need to get the whole community to change ultimately.
After 5 or 6 years of training, a solid base of farmers should have developed, who are fully
persuaded to continue without your presence being necessary. The practising numbers in the
community continue to swell, even after you are no longer there, eventually even extending this
message of hope to neighbouring communities.
This is why you need to carefully select where you are going to train Farming God's Way.

3) Finding the “Man of Peace”
When starting in a community, first find a “man of peace”. You cannot serve a community unless
you have a person on the ground that is trustworthy and will carry the torch in your absence,
preferably displaying the equivalent of an elder’s qualities. This person should be contactable and



                                                 162
be able to help you facilitate in gathering the people for the training times, so he/she needs to be a
person of influence.

4) Free Training to All – No Preferential Treatment
In your extension, ensure that the training is available to all and not just for a select few. You may
get wonderful results with one disciple, but they can get ostracised and persecuted because of
jealousy by their communities and as a result the broader extension will suffer in the long run. Even
if you do discipleship one on one, invite the community to attend teaching times and include them
in farm visits.


5) Size of Extension Sites
We heartily encourage Farming God’s Way training to be taught using the Well Watered Garden
size like a classroom. Here the whole community can come and get all the training they need in
order to be faithful with what God has put in their hands. However, we have seen that by doing
training in a farmer’s field and expanding at least the first two or three rows across the field using
field scale measuring ropes, and then doing the remaining six or seven rows at Well Watered
Garden size, that farmers can really visualise Farming God’s Way more appropriately. Otherwise,
experience has shown that farmers seem to end up planting Well Watered Gardens and think
Farming God’s Way is for small gardens and that is all they ever prepare. On the contrary,
whole-hearted field scale implementation is our objective.
Some Farming God's Way trainers have tried using large scale implementation sites, run by their
organisations, in order to get communities to buy in to Farming God's Way. Although they get good
results on the model site, the actual knock on effect into the community is limited, as it is regarded
as a project, rather than a method. I once tried this on a one hectare extension site in Kenya, which
cost a fortune to manage and the knock on effects into the community were very poor. It is far
more cost effective and empowering to train using a faithful farmer’s field for field scale modelling
or to use small Well Watered Gardens at community sites.

6) Multiple Hits in a Community per Season’s Effectiveness
It is a great advantage if you live in or nearby to the community you are going to train, as you can
break the training sessions down into smaller portions, taking the farmers through a production
timeline from land preparation and planting, to weeding and topdressing right through to harvesting
and post harvest stalk lodging. You must just ensure you give sufficient lead time to the farmers so
that they can implement on their lands what has been taught at the Well Watered Garden site. You
can also break up the Biblical and Management keys, so that the community goes on a journey of
acquiring knowledge of the deeper truths in smaller portions, rather than all together in one batch.
Whether you train into a community more than once per season or not, try not to give an
information overload. For example, if I train a community once per season, I won’t deliver all 6 of
the Biblical keys at one sitting, but rather 2 at a time and bring the fullness of the keys over a
number of years.


7) Timing of Training
The timing of training is very important for effective implementation of Farming God's Way. Too
early in the season and people will be unsure of what you taught by the time it comes to digging
the planting stations; too late and you won’t give them enough lead time to actually prepare their
fields on time or they would have already burnt and ploughed or ridged their fields.
For a November planting region, set a training time between August and early October, with
September being prime time.
For a March planting region, December-February training is best, with January being prime training
time.




                                                 163
8) Importance of Small Groups
I have been surprised about how effective the placing of farmers into small groups is. Encourage a
list of trained farmers to get into groups of around 5 people. These groups are extremely powerful
and should not be underestimated for the peer to peer discipleship and competition aspect. Each
group has a representative who will in turn report to the community Farming God's Way
representative. When training is to be arranged by a trainer, the community representative gets the
message to the group leaders who in turn communicate to their members. They get together to
pray every week, alternating between members’ fields and discipling each other in godliness and
Farming God's Way.


9) Incentives or Handouts of Fertiliser and Seed
Many NGO’s and even church based initiatives endorse the use of incentives such as seed and
fertiliser. However, we do not advocate this. Unless a farmer is persuaded in his or her heart first,
they will not continue in the practise of Farming God's Way when the incentive is finally withdrawn,
as they believe it was the inputs and not Farming God's Way that caused them to succeed. We
also have to search our hearts to discern whether our incentives are putting a yoke on the
community, by making them dependent upon us. God has given to every person, according to their
measure, what they need to succeed and if we truly believe this then we can serve the poor by
taking them on a journey of being faithful with little, so that when they have much they can manage
the increase properly.


10) Field Evaluations
Although field evaluations are not mandatory, they are very useful for discipling of farmers,
reinforcing all the valuable attributes of Farming God's Way and exposing the reality of the
standards. They also provide accountability and help identify areas of weaknesses that need extra
training.
If the farms are not too far apart, we select 10 farmers as representatives of the community
including pastors, good and average farmers, the elderly, widows and orphans.
It is best to evaluate the farmers in their own fields during and at the end of the season. The first
time to evaluate is after thinning and again at harvest time. Evaluate the same farmers for the
duration of the 5/6 years of your repeat training. The manual contains an example evaluation form
for monitoring the selected farmers.


11) Prayer
One must not underestimate the importance of prayer. Bring the area before the Lord in prayer and
ask Him for His Word to be clearly given you, so that you can know where to teach, and who the
man of peace is that you must work alongside. I encourage prayer to be done at regular intervals
such as the start and finish of the Well Watered Gardens, before and after training sessions and
even more importantly, as you disciple individual farmers. I have found that taking the time to do
farm visits and praying for individual farmers and their fields has been very effective at winning
over many people to whole heartedly change to doing Farming God's Way. The poor are incredibly
relational and the personal touch of a field visit and individual prayer goes a long way to establish a
relationship based on sincere love.
Remember to ask your friends, family and colleagues to cover you in prayer as you go and keep
on putting on the full armour of God as your protection, as you are going into enemy territory. He
does not want the yoke of oppression lifted off the lives of the poor.


Summary
Let’s take this message motivated by obedience to the Word of God, rooted in Christ-like
compassion and deliver it with much love for the poorest of the poor to be set free from the yoke of
poverty.

   24.1.      Well Watered Garden Check List
Well Watered Garden Kit

        7.2m measuring rope with 60cm markers
        6m measuring rope with 75cm markers
        Downslope Rope 7-10m
        Large Paper Square
        4 permanent pegs
        20 × 4 inch nails
        1 × matchbox
        2 × 75cm sticks
        1 × 60cm stick
        3 × teaspoons (5ml flat or 8ml heaped)
        3 × tablespoons (12ml flat or 15ml heaped)
        3 × 500ml cups or tins
        Hammer
        1 Cup of good quality maize seed about 350 seeds
        1 Cup of good quality bean seed about 180 seeds
        1.5 litres of woodash
        0.5kg of basal fertiliser - Compound D, DAP or NPK 2.3.2.
        0.5kg of topdress fertiliser - AN or Urea

       Items to get on site
        5 hoes
        1 × 50kg bag of manure
        A large heap of God’s Blanket
        200 litres of water

Experimental Kit
       2 × Runoff trays – 20 litre bottle cut in half with holes drilled in the bottom
       4 × Clear 2 litre containers for runoff experiment
       1 × Clear 2 litre container with small holes for a watering can
       4 × Clear 500ml litre containers
       One 10cm × 15cm sponge cut up into small 2mm pieces; in stocking (ploughed)
       One 10cm × 15cm standard Sponge (unploughed)
       One wide container to catch runoff off bread
       Loaf of bread

       Items to get on site
       10 litre bucket of water for experiments
       2 clods of soil – 1 from ploughed field the other from pristine forest soil or Farming God's
       Way field

Measuring Rope Kit
      50m measuring ropes with 60cm markers
      50m measuring ropes with 75cm markers
      Blank nylon rope
      Plastic bags cut into lots of 1cm wide and 10cm long strips
      Soft binding or copper wire
      Several sets of pliers

24.3.     Recommended Production Timeline – Southern Africa


                                 Maize timeline

              Date                Production                  Time

          01 September            Holing out                6 weeks

           15 October       Liming, Basal dressing

           31 October      Land Preparation finished

        10-25th November     Optimal Planting date       After good rains

          01 December            1st weeding                 7 days

          08 December      Thinning then Topdress 1       @ 2/3 weeks

          15 December              Weeding                   7 days

25. Farming God's Way Resources

Farming God's Way Website: www.farming-gods-way.org

Manuals
All manuals are free downloads from the Farming God’s Way website. These include the Trainer's
Reference Guide, Field Guide, Vegetable Guide, Gardens of Faithfulness and various translations.
English printed stock is available for purchase. See email details below for enquiries.

Farming God's Way Video Training Series:
The video training series is made up of 3 different components:
       1) Main Series: Overview, Biblical Keys, Management, Technology - 18 Videos
         2) Vegetable Series: 31 Vegetable Videos
         3) Gardens of Faithfulness Series: 10 Videos and a Facilitator Program
The training series has been translated into several languages.

Contact info@farming-gods-way.org if you would like to purchase the series on flash drive or
enquire about translations.

Farming God's Way YouTube Channel:
https://www.youtube.com/channel/UCkRvml2KKMpstMwrfgKCVyw

27. Glossary of Terms

Tablespoon:     Heaped - 15ml; Flat -12ml
Teaspoon:       Heaped - 8ml; Flat - 5ml
Acre:           0.405 hectares
Hectare (ha): 100 meters by 100 meters i.e. 10,000 square meters
Hectare:        2.47 acres
kg/ha:          kilograms per hectare
Ton (t):        1,000 kilograms
CAN:            Calcium ammonium nitrate; LAN - Limestone ammonium nitrate
DAP:            Diammonium Phosphate
NPK:            Nitrogen Phosphorus Potassium
